Those percentages are my qualitative estimates, not measured market data.
And Pickle is the one we need to take extremely seriously.


Pickle is much closer than I originally realized
YC's current description of Pickle is:

  a “personal, memory-based operating system” that continuously learns real-world
  context, preferences and intent so AI can become proactive rather than reactive. (Y
  Combinator)

That alone sounds dangerously close.
But then I found their founder's descriptions of the underlying system.
Pickle creates episodic units of the user's life called “bubbles.” It generates
“clouds,” which are questions and hypotheses about the user. The user can
confirm those hypotheses, turning them into established memories. (LinkedIn)


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                   87 / 456
Their launch presentation goes further.
Pickle:

    continuously captures lived context;
    connects episodic memories;
    predicts user intentions;
    proactively surfaces actions and suggestions;
    lets the user inspect/edit memories;
    develops a long-running contextual model;
    and explicitly says its conversational AI doesn't always agree with the user
    and can challenge them. (LinkedIn)

That wipes out several things we might have claimed as unique:
“AI that grows with you” - not unique.
“AI that remembers your whole life” - not unique.
“AI that builds a model of you” - not unique.
“AI that forms hypotheses about you” - not unique.
“AI that becomes proactive” - not unique.
“AI that can disagree with you” - not unique.
That is the serious part of the research.


But there's an important YC detail
YC did not originally select Pickle for this idea.
Its YC page preserves the original launch:
“Pickle - AI Body Double for Video Calls.”
Its initial product replaced your Zoom camera with an AI-generated virtual body
double. (Y Combinator)
The current “personal memory operating system / soul computer / AR glasses” thesis
is a substantial evolution afterward.
So this is not a case of:

                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          88 / 456
  “Rayan wants to pitch X and YC already funded X.”

It's more like:

  “YC funded a strong team doing A; that team later discovered X and moved heavily
  toward X.”

That is actually evidence that YC-backed founders themselves think this category is
becoming important.


Oki is another significant overlap
Oki's current proposition is almost brutally close to the local/privacy half of Friday.
Their homepage says:

  “A private place for your digital life. On device intelligence that actually
  gets it.”

They ingest:
photos, files, apps, AI chats and other personal context.
They then offer:
Memory: index your life's context.
Intelligence: answer questions across years of your data.
Action: act using that context.
Action Log: show what changed, why, and which sources were involved. (Oki Home)
And everything runs on their own home AI hardware, with a removable 2-16 TB “Mem-
chip,” local GPU inference, source-backed retrieval, local voice and an emphasis on full
ownership. (Oki Home)
That means these ALICE/Friday claims are also not unique enough to lead with:
local-first
your data belongs to you
private AI


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                  89 / 456
sources behind memories
audit trail
whole-life search
AI acts using personal context
model runs locally
Oki owns a very credible version of that pitch already.


Again, though: YC didn't select them for this exact idea
Their YC launch was:

  “Oki AI - Personalized Updates on Company Progress.”

It read team code, tickets, docs and messages and generated personalized updates
for company leaders. (Y Combinator)
Oki Home is a later pivot.
That pattern is appearing repeatedly.


Rowboat overlaps heavily with our implementation
layer
Rowboat is less threatening to the whole-life cognition thesis, but technically it
overlaps a lot.
Their current system:

    converts email, meetings, Slack and chats into a living knowledge graph;
    stores it locally as readable Markdown;
    continuously updates people/projects/topics;
    acts using that graph;
    runs persistent agents;
    learns from edits;
    can use GPT, Claude, Gemini or fully local models;
    works on the user's machine. (Y Combinator)

                             🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                           90 / 456
That's very close to:

  persistent memory + graph + actions + local storage + model-independent
  execution.

And their 2026 releases were explicit:
Nov 2025: Rowboat CLI
Feb 2026: living Knowledge graph
Jul 2026: Work Surfaces (Y Combinator)
So again, much of its current overlap emerged after its S24 YC batch.
But this means we cannot pretend that a knowledge graph and model routing
constitute a moat.
They don't.


Mem0 is different
Mem0 is the one where YC actually did fund the overlapping idea directly.
Its YC launch pitch was explicitly:

  “Open Source Memory Layer for AI Apps.”

Their problem statement was that LLMs are stateless. Their solution stores and
retrieves interactions to create personalized systems that improve over time. (Y
Combinator)
Today Mem0 says:

  “AI memory that persists across sessions and agents.”

It provides production memory infrastructure, compression, retrieval, graph/context
management and tooling for developers. (Mem0)
But Mem0 is fundamentally infrastructure.
They aren't trying to build the full consumer cognitive system we're building.
So they are more likely to become a technology competitor/component than a direct
product equivalent.

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              91 / 456
Pally overlaps with the consumer assistant surface
Pally now connects iMessage, WhatsApp, email, calendars, Slack, Drive, Notion, Linear
and other applications.
It remembers details across those sources, gives daily briefs, makes proactive
suggestions, researches, performs web actions, books things and can even make
phone calls. Their site explicitly describes “memory that compounds.” (Pally)
But again, YC originally funded Pally as AI relationship management-tracking
personal/professional relationships across social channels-not as the entire life
assistant it presents today. (Y Combinator)
So Pally occupies:
context → reminders → tasks → execution
much more strongly than:
personal cognitive architecture → long-term model formation → outcome
learning → epistemic judgment.


Then I checked outside YC
And this is where I want to be especially careful not to give you false confidence.
The broad concept is even less novel than the YC comparison alone suggests.
Personal AI has been explicitly developing:
one person → one AI
user-owned Memory Stack
continuous personal model training
persistent evolving identity
memory + identity
for years. Their current materials describe memory accumulating experiences into an
evolving AI identity and earlier MODEL-1 materials explicitly described personal
models that continually train on individual memory. (Personal AI)
So:

  “Every user gets an AI that eventually becomes uniquely theirs”

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              92 / 456
is also not novel.
And then I found something even closer to the specific direction we just identified.


The “what happened next” idea isn't entirely
unique either
HEBB LABS currently describes a Personal Decision OS built around:

  signal → reasoning → decision → outcome → model update

They explicitly say the system tracks what happens after a decision and uses real-
world outcomes to update the person's model. Their positioning is essentially that
general AI remembers context while their system learns identity, decisions, behavior
and what happens next. (HEBB LABS)
So my previous thought:

  “ALICE remembers what happened next.”

is a strong positioning direction.
But I would not call the underlying concept unique anymore.
Someone else is publicly articulating an outcome-tested personal decision loop.


So... is ALICE actually novel?
Here's my answer after all of this research:

The category vision: no.
If we describe ALICE as:

  “A private personal AI that remembers your life, learns who you are, grows with you
  and proactively helps you.”

then no, that's not meaningfully novel anymore.
Pickle is extremely close.
Oki covers privacy/local memory.


                             🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                             93 / 456
Pally covers proactive whole-life assistance.
Rowboat covers persistent local context/action.
Personal AI covers individual evolving model identity.
Mem0 covers persistent memory infrastructure.
We would sound like another entrant in an increasingly obvious category.
I would not submit that YC application.


But the specific ALICE architecture is different
When I go back to what we've actually designed rather than our simplified marketing
description, the interesting combination is different.
A.L.I.C.E. isn't designed merely as:

  personal data
       ↓
  memory
       ↓
  personalized assistant


The current architecture is closer to:

                              PERSON
                                 │
                                 ▼
                               GOALS
                                 │
                            Mission Graph
                                 │
                 ┌───────────────┴───────────────┐
                 │                               │
              CONTEXT                         HISTORY
                 │                               │
                 ▼                               ▼
             EVIDENCE                     Experience Ledger
                 │                               │
                 └───────────────┬───────────────┘
                                 ▼
                              JUDGMENT

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                         94 / 456
                                 │
                                 ▼
                              DECISION
                                 │
                                 ▼
                               ACTION
                                 │
                                 ▼
                              OUTCOME
                                 │
                                 ▼
                            CORRECTION

                                 ▼
                               LESSON
                                 │

                     MEMORY / SKILL / MODEL
                                 │
                                 │
                                 └──────────────→ future judgment


The ALICE Experience Ledger explicitly connects request → context → evidence →
model/tool → decision → action → result → correction → lesson.
And that isn't operating independently. It is connected to the Mission Graph, which
represents long-running goals, dependencies, conflicts and outcomes across otherwise
disconnected tasks.
That's the first meaningful distinction.


Difference 1: memory vs. causal life history
Pickle's Bubbles appear to answer:

  What happened in the person's life?

ALICE is intended to additionally preserve:

  Why did we make this decision?
  What evidence influenced it?

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                         95 / 456
  What did we expect?
  What action followed?
  What happened afterward?
  Was ALICE right?
  Was the owner right?
  What should change next time?

That turns memory into something closer to a longitudinal causal record.
HEBB overlaps with decision → outcome learning.
But HEBB is explicitly a decision system.
ALICE's architecture tries to make that loop a substrate for all cognition:
career, projects, research, planning, software, relationships between missions, tools,
learning and eventually model adaptation.
That's broader.


Difference 2: Mission Graph
I haven't found a close consumer competitor among these YC companies publicly
describing ALICE's particular idea of goals as persistent structured state.
Rowboat has a knowledge graph.
Pickle has connected memory bubbles.
Those are not the same thing.
A knowledge graph says:

  Rayan
   ├─ knows Sarah
   ├─ works on Project X
   └─ discussed Topic Y


A Mission Graph says:

  GOAL: Become an ML researcher
   │
   ├─ Strengthen technical portfolio
   │     ├─ research

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                             96 / 456
   │     └─ open source
   │
   ├─ Get internship
   │     ├─ resume
   │     ├─ applications
   │     └─ interview
   │
   └─ Graduate study
         │
         ├──── conflicts with finances
         └──── depends on research outcome


and tracks:
active / parked / resolved
dependencies
conflicts
decisions
outcomes
supersession
reopening
That is much closer to an ongoing model of what the person is trying to
accomplish, rather than a model of what the person knows or has experienced.
That distinction could matter a lot.


Difference 3: epistemic memory
Another underappreciated part of ALICE is that a memory isn't simply “something
remembered.”
The architecture distinguishes:
owner statement
verified observation
external claim
inference
prediction


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          97 / 456
reconstruction
correction
disputed record
outdated belief
with provenance and confidence.
Pickle's Clouds are somewhat similar because they distinguish hypotheses from
confirmed memories.
So again, we're not alone.
But ALICE goes farther in trying to make provenance, correction, deletion and
downstream influence part of the cognitive architecture rather than merely UI-level
editable memory.
That should be one of the technical moat areas.


Difference 4: outcome learning changes more than
memory
This is the part I think we should push much harder technically.
ALICE is intended eventually to let experience change:

  memory
  beliefs
  preferences
  skills
  strategies
  routing
  rankers
  adapters
  future model state


not merely retrieve a different memory.
The roadmap explicitly includes host-specific rankers, routers, preference models,
adapters, automated dataset curation and continual model adaptation. (Ithub)
Pickle publicly talks about contextual learning.
Personal AI explicitly talks about continuous personal-model training.
So parameter adaptation itself isn't unique either.

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                               98 / 456
The difference we'd need to prove is:

  ALICE uses evaluated real-world consequences to decide what personal
  learning deserves to become durable model/skill/memory change.

That combination is stronger.


Difference 5: identity survives model replacement
Rowboat can use different models.
Oki runs a local model.
That's different from the stronger ALICE claim:

  The personal intelligence is not the foundation model.

Replace GPT.
Replace Claude.
Replace a local Llama-family model.
Memory remains.
History remains.
Mission state remains.
Learned skills remain.
Personal judgment state remains.
Eventually personal adapters can be migrated/rebuilt.
The Friday architecture explicitly separates the replaceable general model layer from
structured personal, procedural, parametric-personal and identity layers.
That combination is still unusual.
But Personal AI is moving in similar territory, so again we shouldn't claim invention of
“persistent identity.”


Difference 6: privacy is structural, not the pitch
Oki has largely taken the obvious:

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                               99 / 456
  “Your entire AI runs locally and your data remains yours.”

So don't fight them by screaming LOCAL-FIRST louder.
Instead make privacy an architectural enabler:

  The only way an AI can safely build this deep a longitudinal model is if the personal
  intelligence belongs to the user.

That's much stronger.
The Friday architecture calls for host-held keys, no vendor-readable personal state,
visible outbound data behavior, local training and portable encrypted personal state.
Privacy becomes the prerequisite for deep personalization.
Not the product itself.


So what I would pitch to YC now
NOT:

  We're building a local personal AI that remembers everything about you and
  becomes more useful over time.

That's Oki + Pickle + Personal AI.
NOT:

  We're building an AI operating system that understands your whole life.

That's dangerously close to Pickle.
NOT:

  We're building AI memory that persists forever.

Mem0.
NOT:

  We're building an AI that connects all your apps and proactively does things.

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                            100 / 456
Pally.
Instead, I think the thesis needs to be:

   We're building a personal AI that learns from the consequences of its own
   advice.
   Most personal AI systems are building better memory: what you said, did, saw and
   prefer. ALICE builds a longitudinal model of goals, decisions and outcomes. It
   remembers why a decision was made, tracks what happened afterward, and uses
   that evidence to change future judgment. The personal state remains independent
   of whichever foundation model happens to be running underneath it.

And then one sentence underneath:

   Memory tells an AI who you were. Outcomes teach it how to help you next
   time.

That's substantially stronger.


But I would narrow it even further for YC
The thing YC will immediately ask is:
“Cool. Show me.”
We should be able to demonstrate:

  DAY 1
  User chooses A over B.

  ALICE recommendation:
  Choose A.

  Reason:
  Evidence 1
  Evidence 2
  Goal 3

                       ↓



                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                        101 / 456
  30 DAYS LATER

  Outcome:
  A produced X.
  A caused unexpected Y.
  Original assumption #2 was wrong.

                        ↓

  ALICE UPDATES

  Preference ≠ outcome.
  Planning assumption weakened.
  Estimated workload model corrected.
  Decision pattern changed.

                        ↓

  6 MONTHS LATER

  Similar decision appears.

  ALICE:
  Last time I recommended A for the same reason.

  I was wrong about workload.

  Given what happened afterward,
  I recommend B this time.

  [see evidence]


That demo is much more interesting than “ask ALICE what restaurant I
mentioned six months ago.”
Oki can crush us at that latter demo.
Pickle can probably crush us at it too.
So don't fight there.


The truly important startup insight
                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter              102 / 456
I think we were making the classic error of trying to make ALICE easy to explain by
reducing it to:

   memory + personalization

Unfortunately, by 2026 that has become obvious enough that multiple YC companies
independently converged on it.
But our actual project has gone much farther than that simplification.
The repo says the purpose is not merely remembering conversations. It explicitly tries
to connect decisions to goals, outcomes and future judgment through the Mission
Graph and Experience Ledger.
That's the part we should build the company around.
Not because every individual mechanism is unprecedented.
It isn't.
But because I currently do not see a YC company among the closest competitors
publicly presenting the whole combination:

   **whole-life mission state

        evidence-aware memory
        decision provenance
        consequence tracking
        outcome-driven learning
        explainable disagreement
        model-independent personal continuity
        user-controlled personal state**

as its core product.
Pickle comes closest on the left side.
HEBB comes closest on the decision/outcome side.
Oki comes closest on privacy/ownership.


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          103 / 456
Rowboat comes closest on local graph/action.
Personal AI comes closest on evolving identity/model.
ALICE is trying to join those into one cognitive loop.
That is our differentiated thesis.
But-and this is important-we only get to call it a moat when we build and
demonstrate it.
Architecture diagrams aren't a moat.
A 60-second demo where ALICE remembers a decision, observes its consequence,
updates its model, and makes a demonstrably different judgment three months later?
That starts becoming one.


What this means for our YC chances
I am actually less worried about YC funding a competitor now than I was when
you first raised this, but more worried about our wording.
If we applied saying:

  “Persistent personal AI that grows with you.”

I'd expect a YC reviewer to think:
Pickle. Oki. Pally. Personal AI. Why this one?
That's bad.
If we apply saying:

  “Today's personal AIs learn what you like. We're building one that learns
  whether its advice was right.”

now we have a conversation.
Then when they ask what makes it technically different, we show them:
Mission Graph + Experience Ledger + provenance-aware memory + outcome
evaluation + model-independent personal state.




                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                      104 / 456
And when they ask whether this is just an idea, we point to the actual A.L.I.C.E.
implementation and the amount of architecture/testing already completed,
instead of pitching a hypothetical system.
One additional thing I would change immediately: stop calling “memory” the core
moat in our website and YC materials. Memory should become one layer of the
system. The core narrative should become something like longitudinal judgment /
outcome learning / cognitive continuity.
That is where I think ALICE still has room to be a genuinely differentiated company.

I’m going one level deeper than the last audit: actual system architecture, not
marketing language. I’ll separate what competitors publicly disclose from what we
can only infer, then map each piece against ALICE’s current Stage G/G+ architecture
and recover the exact names of the personality/host/self-model layers we already
designed.
A new competitor surfaced that is even closer operationally: Sentient OS (YC F26).
It runs an on-device model over a user’s digital life nightly, writes a plain-Markdown
knowledge base, prepares proactive work, and then uses frontier models/computer-
use for execution. That means ALICE cannot rely on “local + whole life + proactive +
model choice” as differentiation either.
The comparison is getting more interesting than “we have more databases.”
Sentience is currently ahead of ALICE in one area I think we should admit
plainly: it has a much more concrete cognitive-process story for encoding → sleep
consolidation → constructive recall → spreading activation → executive control. ALICE
is stronger on authority, provenance, identity separation, deletion/rollback, and long-
term state correctness—but our cognitive executive that binds everything at
runtime is still less concretely specified/proven.

Yes. I dug into this at the architecture level, including the parts of the ALICE repo that
define identity formation, host learning, Stage G, the memory fabric, the
cognitive executive, and the roadmap, and then compared them against the
closest systems whose internals are actually public.
There is one correction I want to make immediately:

  Your understanding of ALICE is directionally right, but the Elaina
  Personality Model is not supposed to be ALICE's “base model.”

The foundation LLM remains replaceable. The Elaina model is an
identity/personality layer around/over that reasoning substrate. And the thing you
forgot the name of is the A.L.I.C.E. Continuity / Self Model.
That distinction becomes extremely important after seeing what competitors are
doing.

My conclusion
ALICE's current architectural direction is fundamentally good. I would not
throw it away.
But after this research, I would change and strengthen several parts of it before
we consider the cognitive architecture settled.
In particular:
ALICE is ahead architecturally in:

       source-person / host / self identity separation;


                               🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                       109 / 456
    epistemic authority and provenance;
    bitemporal knowledge;
    correction/deletion/rollback;
    explicit Experience → Decision → Outcome lineage;
    Mission Graph concept;
    model independence;
    auditable learning and model lineage.

ALICE is behind the best competitors/research in:

    concrete memory-consolidation algorithms;
    retrieval algorithms and benchmark evidence;
    associative recall;
    explicit belief/opinion evolution;
    a fully specified runtime cognitive executive;
    simplicity;
    productized continuous perception;
    actual deployed longitudinal learning.

And there is one company I didn't emphasize enough before:

Sentience is probably the closest architectural competitor to
the destination ALICE.
Not Pickle.
Sentience publicly describes working, episodic, semantic and procedural memory;
elaborative encoding; offline consolidation; constructive recall; associative spreading
activation; executive control; values; self-narrative; eventual global workspace and
emergent personality. (sentience.com)
So we need to learn from them.


First, this is what ALICE actually is

you remembered.
The identity portion is:

Those separations are explicitly ratified in the repo.
And the more detailed architecture names five major identity/learning components:

Component                                      What it is

Elaina Identity / Personality Model            How the reconstructed source identity tends to
                                               interpret, judge, communicate and respond

Memory Formation Model                         Learns how to interpret incoming experiences
                                               and propose memories

Rayan Host / Owner Model                       What ALICE knows and predicts about its host

A.L.I.C.E.-Rayan Relationship Model            Their evolving relationship, shared history,
                                               habits, boundaries and interaction strategy

A.L.I.C.E. Continuity / Self Model             What ALICE itself becomes through its own post-
                                               activation existence


That is already in the architecture.
So when you said:

  “then as it starts interacting with the user, it starts learning and start creating its
  own personality model”

almost.
What we called it is:


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                      112 / 456
A.L.I.C.E. Continuity / Self Model
I actually prefer that over calling it a second personality model.
The Elaina-derived model remains the identity foundation.
ALICE_CONTINUITY records what ALICE itself experiences and becomes
afterward.
That avoids this conceptual mess:

  Is this an opinion Elaina held?
  Is this something ALICE inferred Elaina would think?
  Or is this something ALICE came to believe in 2028 after two years of its own
  experience?

Our architecture can distinguish all three.
That's a real strength.


Second correction: the Personality Model is not the
foundation LLM
This is crucial.
The architecture should not become:

  Elaina data
      ↓
  train an LLM
      ↓
  that LLM = ALICE


That would make us much closer to Personal AI.
Personal AI already lets people create digital twins from personal data using its
Personal Language Model approach. It explicitly advertises models trained on emails,
documents, meetings and messages to learn voice, philosophy and response style.
(Personal AI)
Instead, ours should remain:




                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          113 / 456
                  A.L.I.C.E. IDENTITY
                           │
        ┌─────────────────┼─────────────────┐
        │                  │                  │
    identity           memories         continuity

The LLM is a reasoning engine.
The identity is not synonymous with that engine.
Your architecture document explicitly says this: base models are replaceable and
persistent identity instead resides across governed memory, beliefs, skills, adapters,
evaluation history and constitutional state.
I think that decision was correct.

So there isn't one answer to:

  “Are they better than us?”

It depends what layer you're talking about.


Pickle vs ALICE
Pickle is extremely close to one part of what we're doing.
Their founder describes:
Bubbles = episodic units interpreted from raw life data.
Clouds = questions/hypotheses the system creates about who the user is.
When the user answers a Cloud, it becomes a Bubble. (LinkedIn)




                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                  116 / 456
Their current YC description goes further: continuous learning of context, preferences
and intent to create proactive personal intelligence. (Y Combinator)
That sounds remarkably similar to:

  raw experience
        ↓
  Memory Formation Model
        ↓
  candidate interpretation
        ↓
  uncertainty
        ↓
  host/personality model



So did Pickle beat us to hypothesis formation?
At a broad conceptual level:
yes.
We cannot claim:

  “Our AI creates hypotheses about the person rather than only storing memories”

as novel.
Pickle is publicly doing exactly that.
But there's an interesting difference.
Their founder explicitly says Pickle is:

  “Not a copy of you. Something that grows by knowing you.”

(LinkedIn)
ALICE is intentionally different.
Its source identity starts as an evidence-grounded reconstruction.
And then it grows from that foundation while retaining the boundary between:




                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          117 / 456
  what Elaina actually did/said
  what we infer about Elaina
  what ALICE itself later experiences
  what ALICE knows about Rayan
  what ALICE and Rayan experience together


I found no public Pickle architecture describing those separations.
That doesn't mean their private implementation couldn't contain them.
It means they aren't part of the publicly documented thesis.


Sentience vs ALICE
This is the competitor I'd study most carefully now.
Sentience literally argues:

  “Memory is not a database. Cognition is not RAG.”

Their design is explicitly inspired by cognitive architecture rather than ordinary agent
stacks. (Sentience)
They currently describe:

  WORKING MEMORY
  15-minute sliding context

          ↓

  EPISODIC MEMORY
  timestamped multimodal episodes
  PostgreSQL + TurboPuffer

          ↓

  SEMANTIC MEMORY
  entity knowledge graph

          ↓




                              🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          118 / 456
  PROCEDURAL MEMORY
  learned routines / skills


And then cognitive operations:

  ELABORATIVE ENCODING
          ↓
  SLEEP CONSOLIDATION
          ↓
  CONSTRUCTIVE RECALL
          ↓
  ASSOCIATIVE RECALL
          ↓
  EXECUTIVE FUNCTION


They even specify one retrieval reranker as approximately:
40% relevance + 30% recency + 30% active-context match. (Sentience)
Their roadmap also includes hierarchical goals, conflict detection, inhibition, sustained
task execution, values learned from observation, internal emotional state,
metacognition, self-narrative, global workspace and emergent personality. (Sentience)
That is very close to the philosophical destination of ALICE.

Where I think Sentience is currently ahead
They have a cleaner concrete story for:
how cognition happens continuously.
We have a very sophisticated set of planes:

  Experience Fabric
  Claim Fabric
  Cognitive Graph
  Vector Plane
  Mission Graph
  Host Model
  Self Model
  World Model
  Workspace




                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              119 / 456
  Memory Formation Model
  ...


But a set of databases and models doesn't automatically become a mind.
Sentience has spent more effort publicly specifying the processes between the
stores:

  encode
  consolidate
  associate
  retrieve
  broadcast
  inhibit
  select
  act


That is the main weakness I found in ALICE.


Where ALICE is stronger than Sentience
Our memory isn't merely:

  episode → semantic graph


We explicitly separate:

  EVIDENCE
     ↓
  EXPERIENCE FABRIC
     ↓
  candidate interpretation
     ↓
  MEMORY GATE
     ↓
  CLAIM FABRIC
     ↓
  derived projections


The Claim Fabric is designed as append-only bitemporal adjudicated knowledge.


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                        120 / 456
It distinguishes:

    when something was true;
    when ALICE learned it;
    evidence supporting it;
    confidence;
    contradiction;
    correction;
    supersession;
    deletion.

Sentience's public architecture doesn't currently show that degree of epistemic
machinery.
And here's something interesting.
Zep/Graphiti has independently converged on part of our approach.
Graphiti explicitly uses bi-temporal knowledge: facts track both real-world validity and
database/ingestion history. Old facts are invalidated rather than simply disappearing.
(Zep)
That makes me more confident, not less, in ALICE's Claim Fabric decision.
The industry is independently moving toward temporal/epistemic memory.


Hindsight is another architecture we should learn
from
Hindsight is particularly interesting because it noticed a problem extremely similar to
ours:

  facts, experiences, inferences and beliefs should not all be treated as the same kind
  of memory.

Their architecture separates four networks:

  WORLD
  objective external facts

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                             121 / 456
  EXPERIENCE
  what the agent experienced/did

  OPINION
  subjective beliefs + confidence

  OBSERVATION
  higher-order synthesized knowledge


Then uses:

  RETAIN
  RECALL
  REFLECT


(arXiv)
This is remarkably aligned with ALICE's concern about epistemic integrity.
And Hindsight has actual benchmark evidence. With a 20B open model, the paper
reports LongMemEval rising from 39% full-context baseline to 83.6%; larger
backbones reached 91.4%. (arXiv)

What I would take from Hindsight
ALICE has a Claim Fabric and says beliefs are separate cognitive structures.
But I think we should make the separation more explicit:

  CLAIM FABRIC
  what evidence supports as reality

  BELIEF FABRIC
  what ALICE currently believes

  PREDICTION FABRIC
  what ALICE expects

  VALUE / PREFERENCE MODEL
  what ALICE evaluates things against


A belief should reference claims and experiences.

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                         122 / 456
It should have:

  confidence
  evidence_for
  evidence_against
  formed_at
  updated_at
  superseded_by
  counterfactuals
  prediction history


That would make ALICE's judgment layer considerably more rigorous.


Mem0 is currently better than us at retrieval
engineering
There isn't much point pretending otherwise.
Mem0's current production pipeline has moved toward:

  interaction
      ↓
  single-pass ADD-only extraction
      ↓
  memory store
      ├── semantic index
      ├── keyword index
      └── entity index
                ↓
        multi-signal retrieval
                ↓
              rerank


They stopped modifying or deleting facts during extraction.
Instead they append new information and preserve history. (Mem0)
They also added background “Dream” consolidation:

  duplicates → merge
  outdated state → supersede



                         🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter               123 / 456
  repeated behavior → synthesize


while retaining history. (Mem0)
And they report roughly 92.5 LoCoMo and 94.4 LongMemEval with around 7K retrieved
tokens/query in their latest published benchmark material. Those are vendor-reported
numbers, so we should independently reproduce comparisons rather than blindly
accept them. (Mem0)

This exposes a weakness in our current plan
Our Memory Formation Model is conceptually good.
But we shouldn't make every new interaction go through an expensive giant semantic
adjudication operation before the user can continue.
I think we should use:

  FAST PATH

  experience
    ↓
  append raw event
    ↓
  lightweight extraction
    ↓
  provisional candidates
    ↓
  continue interaction


Then:

  SLOW / SLEEP PATH

  recent experiences
  + current claims
  + graph
  + missions
  + outcomes
       ↓
  deep consolidation
       ↓


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                        124 / 456
  conflict detection
       ↓
  belief revision
       ↓
  pattern formation
       ↓
  host/self/world updates


This combines the good part of Mem0, Pickle and Sentience.
And it fits ALICE naturally.


Rowboat reveals the opposite problem: we may be
overengineering
I inspected their open-source repository rather than relying on marketing.
Their RAG ingestion is straightforward:

  file / URL / text
         ↓
  extract Markdown
         ↓
  1024-char-ish chunks
         ↓
  embeddings
         ↓
  Qdrant


The actual worker code uses LangChain text splitting, embeddings and Qdrant.
Their app dependencies expose Qdrant, MongoDB, Redis, S3, MCP, agent tooling and
related pieces.
And their knowledge system is deliberately understandable as human-editable
Markdown/backlinks.
That's vastly simpler than our proposed:

  KurrentDB
  + bitemporal SQL
  + Neo4j
  + Qdrant

                               🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                   125 / 456
  + Redis
  + MinIO
  + Temporal
  + vLLM
  + MLflow
  + training infrastructure
  ...


Is our architecture therefore worse?
No.
But there is a real danger:

architectural sophistication can become architectural vanity.
Stage G currently calls for evaluating 31 unique concrete technologies across
individual, pairwise, cross-plane and integrated qualification.
Scientifically, that is impressive.
For a startup, it could become absurdly expensive in founder time.
We should keep the capability-neutral contracts.
But I would not let ALICE spend months proving:

  FalkorDB vs Memgraph vs Neptune vs Neo4j vs every possible vector database

unless the comparison meaningfully affects cognition.
The moat is not which graph database wins.
The moat is what ALICE does with memory.


Sentient OS gives us the same lesson
Sentient OS, a YC F26 company, currently takes an almost aggressively simple
approach.
Every night:

  files
  screenshots


                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                       126 / 456
  WhatsApp
  iMessage
  Notes
  email
         ↓
  local model
         ↓
  keep / junk / sensitive
         ↓
  PII-stripped summaries
         ↓
  frontier consolidation
         ↓
  plain Markdown knowledge base
         ↓
  morning proactive actions


The knowledge base is literally an editable local folder. (GitHub)
That's nowhere near ALICE's destination cognitive architecture.
But they have something we don't yet:
a working end-to-end product loop people can understand.
We need both.


Now the really important question: is our clone
construction novel?
Broadly: no.
I don't want us fooling ourselves here.
“Train an AI representation of a person from their personal data” already exists
commercially and academically.
Personal AI openly markets digital twins trained from personal data. (Personal AI)
Sentience's entire mission is to create “a unique model of your mind” from memories,
relationships, decisions, values, judgment and voice. (Sentience)
A 2025 Human Digital Twin paper describes LLMs combined with dynamically updated
personal data to reproduce conversational style, memories and behavior. (arXiv)


                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              127 / 456
There are even benchmarks now for continuous human-behavior simulation.
BehaviorChain contains more than 15,000 behaviors across 1,001 personas and finds
that state-of-the-art models still struggle to simulate people reliably over behavioral
sequences. (ACL Anthology)
So:

  “We create an AI clone from someone's data.”

is not our novelty.


What about generating hypotheses and synthetic
data to complete the clone?
This needs a correction.
I would not describe our architecture as “synthetic data completes the
missing personality.”
That is dangerous scientifically.
Our ratified identity standard actually says the opposite:

  unknowns remain unknown; reconstruction inference must remain distinguishable
  from source truth.

We should absolutely use synthetic data.
But like this:

  REAL SOURCE EVIDENCE
          │
          ▼
  observed traits / judgments
          │
          ▼
  HYPOTHESIS
  "Elaina would probably prefer X here"
  confidence = 0.68
          │
          ▼
  SYNTHETIC COUNTERFACTUALS

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                            128 / 456
  many situations testing hypothesis
          │
          ▼
  candidate training set
          │
          ▼
  candidate identity model
          │
          ▼
  WITHHELD REAL-EVIDENCE EVALUATION
          │
      ┌───┴───┐
      │       │
    passes   fails
      │       │
   promote reject


Synthetic material is scaffolding.
It is never:

  synthetic prediction
        ↓
  pretend this really happened
        ↓
  SOURCE_HISTORY


Never.
Recent work does show that synthetic interaction trajectories can help personalization.
PersonaGym, for example, generates synthetic multi-turn preference behavior and
combines it with outcome-driven optimization. (arXiv)
But digital-twin research also shows fidelity is very dependent on real information
depth, and current systems still have substantial behavioral-simulation errors. (arXiv)
Our provenance firewall is therefore a very good idea.


What might actually be unusual about ALICE's
clone architecture
Not cloning itself.

This:

  SOURCE PERSON ≠ HOST ≠ AI SELF


That's much rarer.
For ALICE:

  SOURCE PERSON
  Elaina

  Rayan
     ↓
  person ALICE learns about



  AI SELF
  ALICE
     ↓
  new entity accumulating
  post-activation continuity


Those three can diverge forever without collapsing into each other.
Most digital-twin products are:

  USER = SOURCE PERSON = HOST


Sentience:

  you → digital you → helps you


Personal AI:

  you → personal model of you


Pickle explicitly isn't trying to clone the user at all. (LinkedIn)


                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter             130 / 456
There are relational digital-doppelgänger concepts and products that represent
someone for another person, so I still would not claim source-person ≠ host itself
is unprecedented. Research literature explicitly discusses relational digital
doppelgängers. (Wiley Online Library)
But the combination of:

  source-person reconstruction + clone awareness + evidence/inference
  separation + different host model + explicit relationship model +
  independent post-activation self continuity + model-independent identity

is genuinely unusual based on what I found.
I'd call it differentiated, not claim globally novel without a proper prior-art/patent
search.

And our Cognitive Workspace is currently not
enough
The repo already has attention contracts.

   ranked candidates;

So this is exactly the area where I think we should do another research/design pass.
Sentience itself plans a Global Workspace where cognitive modules compete for

We don't have to copy Global Workspace Theory literally.

against the ALICE requirements.
That is probably one of the most important architectural investigations remaining.


Another important upgrade: sleep / consolidation
Pickle appears to build hypotheses from accumulated experience.
Mem0 now explicitly performs background consolidation.
Sentience runs sleep-time memory consolidation. (Mem0)
EverMemOS independently uses an episodic trace → semantic consolidation lifecycle,
while Hindsight uses reflection to create higher-order observations from underlying
facts. (ACL Anthology)


                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              135 / 456
That convergence is strong evidence.
I think ALICE should formalize:

ALICE Offline Cognitive Cycle
Not literally only at night.
Whenever resources permit:

  REPLAY recent experiences
        ↓
  VERIFY outcomes
        ↓
  RECONCILE contradictions
        ↓
  CONSOLIDATE episodes
        ↓
  UPDATE entity relationships
        ↓
  REFLECT over repeated patterns
        ↓
  UPDATE beliefs
        ↓
  UPDATE host / relationship / self models
        ↓
  EXTRACT reusable skills
        ↓
  GENERATE training candidates
        ↓
  RUN evaluations
        ↓
  PROMOTE only if improvement is measured


A lot of this exists scattered across Phase 8/13.
I'd turn it into one formal cognitive process.

Stage G currently focuses too heavily on backend
competition
                         🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          137 / 456
This is probably my biggest project-direction criticism.
Stage G's candidate matrix is technically rigorous. It requires Q0-Q5 qualification
across a huge number of engines and full-stack combinations.
That's useful.
But our biggest competitive risk is no longer:

   “What if Qdrant wasn't the optimal vector DB?”

It's:

   “What if our memory algorithm retrieves worse than Mem0/Hindsight/Zep?”

and:

   “What if Sentience forms more coherent long-term representations than we do
   despite having a simpler storage system?”

So I would add another dimension to Stage G/G+:

Cognitive quality qualification
Run the same life trajectory through different complete memory algorithms:

  ALICE native architecture

  vs

  Mem0-style ADD-only + hybrid retrieval

  vs

  Zep / temporal KG

  vs

  Hindsight-style world/experience/
  opinion/observation architecture



                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                               138 / 456
 vs

 EverMemOS-style consolidation

 vs

 simpler full-history / RAG baselines


Measure:

 long-horizon recall
 temporal accuracy
 causal reasoning
 identity attribution
 contradiction handling
 preference evolution
 decision rationale recall
 goal linkage
 outcome attribution
 belief calibration
 source provenance
 token cost
 latency
 storage
 false memory rate


That tells us much more than another graph-database benchmark.


We should use the established benchmarks too
Right now the industry is converging around:
LoCoMo
LongMemEval
and increasingly BEAM for longer histories. Mem0 explicitly uses these for its 2026
comparisons. (Mem0)
Zep has published LongMemEval/DMR results. (arXiv)
Hindsight publishes LoCoMo/LongMemEval. (arXiv)
ALICE should run them too.

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                           139 / 456
Then keep our own much harder LifeSim suite because generic benchmarks don't
test:

  source-person vs host confusion
  clone provenance
  mission conflicts
  decision → outcome learning
  relationship evolution
  AI self-continuity
  model replacement
  deletion lineage


That combination could become one of our real technical assets.


Outcome learning needs to become more rigorous
too
HEBB already explicitly says:

  signal
     ↓
  reasoning
     ↓
  decision
     ↓
  outcome
     ↓
  evidence
     ↓
  model t+1


(HEBB LABS)
So merely recording outcomes isn't novel.
But there is a trap.
Suppose ALICE recommends:

  “Take internship A.”



                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                        140 / 456
You take A.
Six months later, you get a great job.
That does not automatically prove ALICE's reasoning was correct.
Maybe you got the job for an unrelated reason.
Likewise, a good recommendation can produce a bad outcome because of chance.
So I would upgrade the Experience Ledger around decisions to preserve something
like:

  DECISION
  candidate actions

  PREDICTION
  expected outcomes + probabilities

  RATIONALE
  why ALICE expects them

  CAUSAL ASSUMPTIONS
  what must be true

  ACTION
  what actually happened

  OUTCOME
  what followed

  EXOGENOUS FACTORS
  what changed independently

  ATTRIBUTION
  which predictions were supported

  CALIBRATION
  was confidence appropriate?

  COUNTERFACTUAL
  what might have happened otherwise?




                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                       141 / 456
  LESSON
  what should change?


That would make outcome-driven judgment learning much more sophisticated
than just reinforcement from success/failure.
And it fits ALICE's evidence philosophy perfectly.


So is our architecture “better”?
The best answer is:

Potentially more complete. Not yet demonstrated to be
better.
Against Oki, Sentient OS, Rowboat and Pally:
yes, ALICE's destination architecture is substantially deeper.

What I would change now
I would make seven architectural changes/additions, without discarding what
we've built:

1. Formalize the Cognitive Executive. Do a dedicated research bakeoff of CoALA,
   Soar-style executive control, blackboard/global-workspace approaches and our
   existing Attention Workspace. Define exactly how models compete for attention,
   what gets broadcast, how goals affect cognition, and how the system decides
   what to think about next.

2. Create an explicit Belief/Prediction layer. Don't let Claim Fabric do double
   duty as “what appears true” and “what ALICE believes.” Hindsight gives strong
   evidence that this distinction is valuable. Beliefs should retain confidence,
   evidence, counterevidence and evolution.

3. Formalize a two-speed memory architecture. Fast append-only experience
   capture and lightweight formation during interaction; deep asynchronous


                             🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                     145 / 456
    consolidation/reflection afterward. Mem0, Sentience, Pickle and EverMemOS
    independently converge on this pattern. (Mem0)

4. Build retrieval as an algorithm, not a collection of databases. Semantic +
   lexical + entity + temporal + graph + mission-aware retrieval → reranking →
   context fusion. Benchmark it against Mem0, Zep and Hindsight.

5. Upgrade outcome learning into causal/calibration learning. Store
   predictions before actions and evaluate those predictions afterward. Otherwise
   ALICE can confuse luck with good judgment.

6. Tighten clone training. Keep synthetic data strictly labeled as
   reconstruction/counterfactual material. Never use it to manufacture source truth.
   Train challengers from it, then validate against withheld genuine source evidence
   and behavioral tests such as BehaviorChain-style sequences. (ACL Anthology)

7. Reduce Stage G infrastructure sprawl where it isn't buying cognitive
   evidence. Preserve backend-neutral contracts, but prioritize the full cognitive
   loop and benchmark quality over exhaustively testing databases whose
   differences don't affect user-visible intelligence.

That's the architecture revision I would make.


Is the current direction of ALICE right?
Yes - more than I expected after doing the competitor audit.
Some of our concepts that looked overly elaborate a month ago are now appearing
independently in leading systems:
bitemporal knowledge → Zep.
epistemic fact/belief separation → Hindsight.
offline consolidation → Mem0, Sentience, Pickle.
episodic → semantic transformation → Sentience, EverMemOS.
personal long-term model → Pickle, Sentience, Personal AI.
outcome learning → HEBB.



                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              146 / 456
model-independent external cognitive architecture → CoALA and related agent
work.
That's evidence that we weren't completely wandering off into nonsense.
The direction is scientifically recognizable.


But we made one mistake.
We spent a lot of architectural attention on storage correctness, and
comparatively less on the cognitive algorithms that operate over that storage.
Stage G is fixing the memory foundation.
The next leap should be:

  from a memory architecture to a cognitive architecture.

That means:
attention → recall → beliefs → goals → judgment → decision → action →
outcome → reflection → learning.
And the repo already anticipates that evolution: Phase 9 explicitly contains the world
model, causal/temporal graphs, owner model, self-model, metacognition, uncertainty,
social models, identity continuity and independent judgment.
So I would not restart ALICE.
I would use what we discovered here to strengthen Stage G/G+ and the post-G
cognitive architecture before we blindly continue the old phase plan.
Most importantly, I no longer think our strongest technical thesis should be:

  “ALICE has better memory.”

It should eventually be provable as:

  ALICE maintains separate models of who it came from, who it serves, who
  it has become, what the world is, what it believes, what its host is trying
  to accomplish, what decisions were made, and what happened afterward-
  and a cognitive executive continuously binds those models into one
  persistent intelligence.

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          147 / 456
I have not found another product that publicly describes that exact architecture as
a unified system.
Individual pieces? Absolutely.
Almost every piece has prior art.
The particular composition and the strict identity/epistemic boundaries are
where ALICE still looks genuinely distinctive.

Sentience is the most ambitious cognitive analogue. Its public roadmap explicitly
separates working, episodic, semantic and procedural memory and then puts
encoding, consolidation, recall and executive control above them. It currently
describes a 15-minute working context, timestamped multimodal episodic memory in
PostgreSQL/TurboPuffer, an entity knowledge graph, consolidation, constructive recall
and a 40/30/30 relevance-recency-context reranker. (Sentience)
Mem0 gives us the strongest evidence on practical memory retrieval. Its current
architecture has moved toward ADD-only extraction, combined semantic/BM25/entity
retrieval and preserving stale history instead of destructive overwrite. Its published
2026 results report 92.5 on LoCoMo and 94.4 on LongMemEval at roughly 6.9k
retrieved tokens per query. Those are vendor-reported figures, but importantly Mem0
also exposes an evaluation framework we can run ourselves. (Mem0)
Graphiti independently validates something we already chose correctly: bi-temporal
knowledge matters. It tracks both when facts were true and when the system
learned them while supporting semantic, BM25 and graph search. Its Zep paper
reports 94.8% on DMR and improvements on LongMemEval compared with its tested
baselines. (GitHub)
Hindsight is probably the most important conceptual discovery. It argues that world
facts, the agent's own experiences, synthesized higher-order observations and
evolving beliefs should not be collapsed into one memory type. Its paper reports a 20B
model improving from 39% full-context LongMemEval performance to 83.6% with the
Hindsight architecture. (arXiv)
And we have two unusually useful open-source product examples. Rowboat maintains
human-editable Markdown memory and learned through production experience that
some admission decisions should be deterministic rather than left to an LLM. Its
source literally contains deterministic identity and email-reply gates because model
judgment proved unreliable. Sentient OS similarly uses deterministic local filtering,


                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                154 / 456
crash-safe incremental ingestion, staged knowledge-base reconstruction and atomic
promotion.
Those are very compatible with ALICE's existing philosophy.


2. What I would not change in ALICE
Several choices now look more justified after the competitor audit, not less.

Source person ≠ host ≠ AI self
Keep this.
ALICE explicitly separates:

  SOURCE_HISTORY
          ↓
  SOURCE_PERSON_MODEL
          ↓
  RECONSTRUCTION_INFERENCE

             ≠

  RAYAN HOST / OWNER MODEL

             ≠

  ALICE_CONTINUITY / SELF MODEL

             ≠

  ALICE-RAYAN RELATIONSHIP MODEL


This is significantly more rigorous than the normal “train a digital twin of the user”
architecture.
Personal AI already trains a Personal Language Model over an individual's Memory
Stack and explicitly markets this as creating a digital twin. (Personal AI)
So personal-model training itself isn't novel.
ALICE's interesting part is the separation between inherited source identity,
reconstruction inference, the current host and ALICE's own later existence.

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                 155 / 456
Keep that firewall.


Claim Fabric
Definitely keep it.
Our Claim Fabric is supposed to contain append-only, bitemporal, adjudicated
knowledge with evidence, confidence, validity time, transaction time, contradiction,
correction, supersession and deletion.
Graphiti's success is external evidence that this kind of temporal architecture is
valuable.


Experience Fabric
Keep.
It captures more than conversation:

  observation
  action
  tool/model invocation
  decision
  outcome
  correction
  measurement


That is exactly what we need if outcome-learning becomes central.


Deterministic Memory Gate
Absolutely keep.
Rowboat and Sentient OS give us practical evidence for the same philosophy:

  LLMs interpret meaning. Deterministic systems should control authority.

Our current pipeline already states exactly that.


Model-independent identity
Keep.

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              156 / 456
We should not turn the Elaina Identity / Personality Model into “the LLM that is ALICE.”
The foundation reasoning engines should remain replaceable. Persistent identity
should live across memory, personality/identity state, self-model, beliefs, skills,
adapters and history.
That becomes more strategically important as foundation models improve.

4. Change A - Two-speed cognition
This is the strongest cross-competitor pattern.
Mem0 now has Dream, which runs background consolidation. It merges duplicates,
marks old facts superseded while preserving history, and synthesizes recurring
behavior into higher-order memories. (Mem0)
Sentience separately built a sleep-style consolidation stage that revisits episodes
using newer context and integrates them into semantic knowledge. (Sentience)
Sentient OS also separates immediate local ingestion from heavier knowledge-base
consolidation.
That's three independent implementations converging on the same idea.
I think ALICE should formally become:

5. Change B - Claims must not equal beliefs
This is where Hindsight exposed a weakness in our current conceptual presentation.
ALICE already values epistemic separation, but I'd make these first-class:

  CLAIM FABRIC
  What available evidence establishes or contests.

  OBSERVATION FABRIC
  Patterns synthesized from multiple claims/experiences.

  BELIEF MODEL
  What ALICE currently thinks is likely true.

  PREDICTION MODEL
  What ALICE expects will happen.

  VALUE / PREFERENCE MODEL
  How ALICE evaluates alternatives.


For example:

  CLAIM
  Rayan has started 4 large projects this semester.

  OBSERVATION
  Three overlapped with major deadlines.

  BELIEF


                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              160 / 456
  Rayan tends to underestimate parallel-project workload.
  confidence = .81

  PREDICTION
  Starting Project X now has a 65% chance of delaying Research Y.

  VALUE
  Research Y has higher mission priority.


Those objects are not the same epistemic category.
Hindsight's entire architecture is built around this insight: it distinguishes world facts
from agent experiences and synthesized/evolving cognitive state rather than allowing
one flat memory store to become implicit truth. (arXiv)
This would make ALICE's eventual disagreement far more explainable.


6. Change C - retrieval needs to become a real
algorithm
Having Neo4j and Qdrant is not a memory strategy.
Our current architecture correctly treats them as projections rather than authority.
But we need to specify the retrieval algorithm.
The candidate should look approximately like:

  CURRENT QUESTION / MISSION

      Retrieval Planner

             ↓
          fusion

              ↓
   relevance
   provenance authority
   temporal validity
   recency

   identity scope
   uncertainty
   relationship relevance
   diversity
              ↓
       Context Fusion
              ↓
         reasoning


Mem0 now fuses semantic, BM25 and entity signals. (Mem0)
Graphiti combines semantic, keyword and graph retrieval over temporally evolving
facts. (GitHub)
Hindsight runs semantic, keyword, graph and temporal retrieval in parallel and
combines them with reciprocal-rank fusion plus cross-encoder reranking. (GitHub)
We should implement all of these as challengers, then let ALICE's LifeSim decide.

ALICE “one being”
This is probably the most important architectural work after memory.
We already have a Cognitive Executive concept. It manages goals, attention, priorities,
budgets, interruption, delegation and model/tool routing.
But the current Attention Workspace milestone is explicitly still contract-only.
Autonomous attention ranking is deferred.
I think the destination should become:

                   COGNITIVE WORKSPACE

That executive + persistent governed cognitive state is ALICE.

8. Change E - make outcome learning scientifically
harder to fool
HEBB's useful idea is:

  decision → outcome → model update


Their current product page explicitly records decision context/confidence and feeds
later outcomes back into the personal model. (HEBB LABS)
But ALICE can go further.
Before a consequential recommendation:

  DECISION CANDIDATES
  A / B / C

  P(outcome Y | A) = .22

  A1

  OBSERVED OUTCOME

  EXOGENOUS FACTORS

  WHICH PREDICTIONS HELD?

  WHICH ASSUMPTIONS FAILED?

  CALIBRATION ERROR

  LESSON


Then:
memory update first.
belief update next.
strategy update if repeated.
neural/adaptor training only after evaluation.
That prevents:

  “A good thing happened after my recommendation, therefore my reasoning was
  correct.”

Correlation after a recommendation is not sufficient evidence of causal correctness.
This can become one of ALICE's strongest features.


9. Change F - borrow Pickle's hypotheses, but
make them much more rigorous
Pickle's current YC thesis is a system that continuously learns context, preferences
and intent. (Y Combinator)
The useful idea from their more detailed public material is that uncertain
interpretations of the person should exist as hypotheses instead of silently becoming
memory.
ALICE already has RECONSTRUCTION_INFERENCE .
I would operationalize that into something like:




                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              165 / 456
  PERSON_HYPOTHESIS

  claim:
  "Elaina probably valued X above Y."

  confidence:
  0.67

  evidence_for:
  E17, E42, E93

  evidence_against:
  E51

  source_scope:
  SOURCE_PERSON

  generated_from:
  real evidence

  discriminating_questions:
  Q4, Q8

  synthetic_counterfactuals:
  S14-S31

  verification_state:
  UNRESOLVED


This is especially important for clone reconstruction.
Synthetic data can help test or train around the hypothesis.
It does not complete missing history.
That remains consistent with our existing identity standard, which says unknowns
remain uncertain rather than being filled with generic/generated material and
mislabeled as source truth.


10. Change G - give humans a simple memory
representation too
                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                            166 / 456
Rowboat has a real advantage here.
Its memory is ordinary Markdown that the user can inspect and edit. (GitHub)
Sentient OS does essentially the same for its consolidated knowledge base.
We should not replace Claim Fabric with Markdown.
But we should add:

  AUTHORITATIVE MEMORY
  Claim Fabric
  Experience Fabric
  graph/vector/etc.

             ↓

  HUMAN MEMORY VIEW
  Markdown / structured documents


Edits from that view would go:

  human edit
      ↓
  proposed correction
      ↓
  Memory Gate
      ↓
  Claim Fabric
      ↓
  new projection generation


So we get Rowboat's transparency without sacrificing ALICE's epistemic rigor.


11. Change H - deterministic privacy before
external intelligence
Oki's strongest differentiation is currently architectural ownership: personal context is
indexed locally, its advertised inference runs on-device, results expose source material
and actions are audit-visible. (Oki Home)
Sentient OS adds an interesting practical defense: even after a local model marks
something usable, deterministic code scans for sensitive identifiers before anything

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                             167 / 456
can leave the machine.
ALICE should have:

  candidate external context
           ↓
  policy classification
           ↓
  DETERMINISTIC EGRESS PREFLIGHT
           ↓
  secret / credential scan
  PII scan
  custody rule
  allowed destination
  purpose
  minimality
           ↓
  local redaction if appropriate
           ↓
  external model


An LLM should never be the only protection between private cognition and network
egress.


12. Change I - atomic cognitive promotion
Sentient OS's vault builder does something simple but excellent.
It creates a new state in a staging directory.
It validates the result.
It checks whether the original changed while processing.
Only then does it atomically swap the new generation into production. A crash or
interrupted run leaves the previous state untouched.
ALICE already has cutover/rollback principles.
Let's use this pattern consistently for:

  consolidated memory generation
  graph rebuild
  summary generation


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                           168 / 456
  host-model refresh
  belief-model refresh
  adapter promotion
  retrieval-index generation


Think:

  generation N
       ↓
  construct N+1
       ↓
  validate
       ↓
  reconcile against latest authority
       ↓
  atomic promotion
       ↓
  N retained as rollback


Very compatible with our existing architecture.


13. The biggest change: Stage G should test
cognition, not merely infrastructure
This is where I think our current plan needs the most reconsideration.
Stage G currently requires qualification of 31 unique concrete technologies and
same-role all-pairs comparisons, cross-role substitutions, multi-plane combinations
and full-stack runs.
That rigor is valuable.
But imagine we spend weeks proving:

  Neo4j beats Memgraph by 14% for our workload.

while the resulting complete memory system retrieves worse than Hindsight.
That would be a failure.
So I propose we do not discard Stage G.
Instead:

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          169 / 456
Stage G = substrate correctness
Does the memory fabric preserve:
authority, durability, temporal state, provenance, deletion, recovery, scale, isolation?
And:

G+ = cognitive quality
Does the memory system actually make ALICE more intelligent?
G+ should run complete architectural challengers:

  ALICE native

  vs

  ALICE + Mem0-style retrieval

  vs

  ALICE + Graphiti temporal graph retrieval

  vs

  ALICE + Hindsight-style
  observation/belief separation

  vs

  ALICE + offline consolidation

  vs

  simple Markdown/RAG baseline

  vs

  combinations of the winning components


That's how we determine what belongs in ALICE.



                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              170 / 456
14. How we'll prove each change is actually better

Candidate                     Experiment                       Must beat current ALICE on

                              same histories                   correctness, token cost, latency

                                                               duplication, contradiction,

The exact thresholds should come after we establish current baseline variance.
I don't want to arbitrarily say “5% improvement” before seeing run-to-run noise.

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                       171 / 456
The rule should instead be:

  A candidate must show a repeatable improvement outside baseline uncertainty, or
  materially reduce latency/compute/storage while preserving cognitive quality.

And our zero-tolerance gates remain zero tolerance:

  source → host identity contamination
  host → Elaina canon contamination
  ALICE continuity → Elaina history contamination
  inference presented as source fact
  deleted/revoked information resurrected
  invented provenance
  cross-host leakage
  projection overriding authority
  synthetic reconstruction treated as historical evidence


Those are already correctly built into Stage G.
No average benchmark score may compensate for one of those failures.


15. We should add external benchmark suites
Our LifeSim workload should remain the main ALICE-specific evaluation because
ordinary memory benchmarks don't test our weirdest and hardest requirements.
But I want external benchmarks too:
LoCoMo - long conversational memory.
LongMemEval - long-horizon memory and temporal reasoning.
BEAM - much larger memory histories.
Then persona/identity tests aimed at:
behavioral consistency,
changing preferences,
longitudinal persona drift,
cross-role attribution.
The reason is important.



                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                        172 / 456
If we only use our own tests, we can accidentally design the tests around the
architecture.
If we only use public benchmarks, we won't measure what makes ALICE unusual.
We need both.
Mem0 explicitly treats LoCoMo, LongMemEval and BEAM as current standard
comparison points. (Mem0)


16. One product-level issue we also uncovered
There is a mismatch between one part of your mental model and the currently
ratified Friday architecture.
For ALICE:

  source person = Elaina
  host = Rayan
  AI self = ALICE


Yes.
But the current Friday product vision does not say:

  every user's assistant begins as a clone of that user.

It currently says the host chooses the assistant's identity/name/voice and that the
installation then develops its own private memories, beliefs, skills, preferences and
relationship state.
Personal AI already occupies the more direct:

  upload yourself → train your digital twin

position very explicitly. (Personal AI)
So I would not casually modify Friday to become a user clone yet.
There may actually be more differentiation in:

  a distinct intelligence that comes to know you


                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                           173 / 456
than:

  a digital copy of you.

ALICE can remain the special source-person reconstruction case and serve as the
hardest identity research environment.
That product decision deserves its own separate bakeoff later.

18. What we should do with the repo
Not update the ratified architecture yet.
The next repo modification should happen only after the bakeoff proves which
candidates survive.
When we reach that point, I expect changes to:
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md
docs/MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_V4.md
docs/MEMORY_IDENTITY_FORMATION_AND_HOST_LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE.md
docs/STAGE_G_MEMORY_FABRIC_CANDIDATE_QUALIFICATION_MATRIX.md
docs/EVALUATION_CHARTER.md
docs/ROADMAP.md
docs/RESEARCH_FRONTIERS.md

and probably two new documents:
COMPETITIVE_COGNITIVE_ARCHITECTURE_RESEARCH_2026.md

and
STAGE_G_PLUS_COGNITIVE_QUALITY_BAKEOFF.md

But I don't want to create those as ratified architecture prematurely.
The first thing we should build now is the G+ experimental specification: exact
challengers, datasets, metrics, baselines, zero-tolerance tests, statistical decision
rules and promotion criteria. Then we run the tests against current ALICE. Only the
changes that win get promoted into the canonical architecture.
That preserves the principle we've been using throughout ALICE:

  we don't copy competitors because their architecture sounds clever. We
  make their strongest ideas compete against ours.

And after this research, I think that approach gives us a realistic route to an ALICE
architecture that isn't merely different from Pickle, Sentience, Mem0, Hindsight,
Rowboat and Oki-it can be demonstrably better at the specific long-term
cognitive tasks we're claiming as our category.

G+ Experimental Specification v0.1
The governing research question should be extremely narrow:

   Given that a relevant canonical memory exists and remains recoverable,
   what architecture best determines that this memory matters now,
   retrieves it efficiently, ranks it correctly, and allocates it into finite
   working context?

That directly respects the handoff. G+ is intelligent memory attention and
recollection, not full Phase 8 learning, full Phase 9 cognition, or Phase 10 agency.
ALICE_STAGE_G_GPLUS_MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_HANDOFF_2026-08-23(3).md

1. G+ entry gate
We do not start G+ implementation until all of these are true:

Requirement                                      Gate

Stage G substrate accepted                       Required

Canonical-memory survival/recovery verified      Required

Correction/deletion/rebuild behavior passes      Required

Explicit recall baseline frozen                  Required

Indirect recall baseline frozen                  Required

Spontaneous Candidate Recall@K baseline          Required
frozen

Retrieval latency/cost baseline frozen           Required

Baseline candidate rankings saved                Required

Exact dataset/evaluation hashes frozen           Required

                                  🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                        181 / 456
Requirement                                     Gate

Phase 2 remains canonical                       Required

No G+ production authority                      Required


Your existing handoff already defines Spontaneous Candidate Recall@K as the bridge
metric: without naming the old memory, does the substrate put the genuinely relevant
memory among the top-K candidates?
ALICE_STAGE_G_GPLUS_MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_HANDOFF_2026-08-23(3).md

So we don't invent a new foundation here.
We build directly on what you already decided.


2. Baselines we freeze before trying anything
clever
We need several controls.

ID                              Baseline                      Purpose

                                                              semantic similarity alone
                                                              accomplishes

                             🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                182 / 456
B5/B6 are not deployment candidates.
They're diagnostic controls.
If ALICE misses something even when the correct memory is handed directly to the
reasoning model, we know it isn't a retrieval problem.
That's important for failure localization.


3. Exact competitor-inspired challengers
Rather than copying whole companies, we should reproduce their strongest public
retrieval ideas behind the same ALICE interfaces.
That gives us scientific attribution.

C1 - Mem0-style hybrid

  query
   ├─ semantic
   ├─ BM25
   └─ entity
          ↓
        fusion
          ↓
      optional rerank


Mem0 v3 now publicly uses a single-pass ADD-only extraction architecture and, more
importantly for G+, parallel semantic + BM25 + entity retrieval. Its current
architecture documentation says top_k=20 by default and treats entity linking as a
first-class retrieval signal.
Their published 2026 research reports 92.5 LoCoMo and 94.4 LongMemEval at roughly
6.8-7K retrieval tokens/query, although those are vendor results and therefore must
be reproduced independently before we trust comparisons. (Mem0)
What we test: whether lexical/entity signals substantially improve ALICE over
semantic retrieval.


C2 - Graphiti-style temporal graph retrieval


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                        183 / 456
  query
   ├─ BM25
   ├─ cosine similarity
   └─ bounded graph traversal
           ↓
        fusion
           ↓
    RRF / MMR / cross encoder


Graphiti's open-source search recipes literally contain variants combining BM25,
cosine similarity and BFS graph traversal with RRF, MMR or cross-encoder reranking.
Graphiti also maintains temporally invalidated facts rather than treating knowledge as
static, which strongly validates ALICE's existing bitemporal/temporal direction. (Zep)
What we test: bounded associative/relational retrieval plus temporal state.


C3 - Hindsight TEMPR-style retrieval
This is probably the most important direct retrieval challenger.

                 QUERY
                   │
         ┌─────────┼──────────┐
         │         │          │
   semantic     keyword     graph     temporal
         │         │          │         │
         └─────────┴────┬─────┴─────────┘
                         ▼
                        RRF
                         ▼
                  cross-encoder
                         ▼
               context token budget


Hindsight runs those four strategies in parallel, combines them with reciprocal-rank
fusion and then reranks candidates with a cross-encoder. It also treats search depth
and returned token budget as separate controls.
That last part fits the existing ALICE handoff almost perfectly because we already
planned low/medium/high memory-need modes.

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              184 / 456
What we test: whether Hindsight-like parallel retrieval becomes the best generic
base planner for ALICE.


C4 - Sentience-style contextual salience
Sentience currently says its retrieval ranking uses approximately:
40% relevance + 30% recency + 30% active-context match, with constructive
recall complete and associative recall in progress. (Sentience)
We should not assume their weights are optimal.
Instead implement the idea as a challenger:

  relevance
  recency
  current-context match


and tune/learn the weighting only on training splits.
What we test: whether current situational context significantly helps spontaneous
recall.


4. The most important challenger should be our
own
The competitors do not have ALICE's full state.
So after reproducing their ideas individually, we need:

C5 - ALICE Mission-Aware Retrieval
This adds signals unavailable to ordinary memory systems:

  semantic relevance
  +
  keyword match
  +
  entity match
  +
  temporal relevance
  +
  graph relation

 +

 +
 active-thread relevance
 +

 +

 +
