 +
 unresolved-event salience
 +
 identity scope
 +
 outcome relevance


That becomes our candidate differentiator.
For example:

 Message:
 "I'm nervous about Tuesday."

 Generic semantic retrieval:
 Tuesday
 nervousness
 calendar events

 ALICE retrieval:
 Tuesday
    ↓
 active interview mission
    ↓
 previous interview experience
    ↓
 specific recruiter/person
    ↓
 past preparation mistakes
    ↓
 current unresolved action


                         🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter   186 / 456
That is where ALICE can legitimately outperform generic memory infrastructure.
The handoff already anticipated active unresolved threads as first-class salience and
explicitly planned mission, entity, temporal, graph, episodic and semantic paths. So
this is not a new direction invented from competitors; the competitor research gives
us better algorithms for implementing a direction ALICE already had.
ALICE_STAGE_G_GPLUS_MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_HANDOFF_2026-08-23.md

  marginal relevance gain

  latency budget

  graph branching factor
  uncertainty


We specifically measure graph explosion.
Being able to traverse ten thousand related nodes isn't intelligence.
Knowing when to stop is.


6. Automatic recall and deliberate recollection
must be separate
This decision in the handoff is excellent.


Automatic Recall
Needs to be fast.

  message
   ↓
  situation interpretation
   ↓
  low-cost multipath search
   ↓
  rerank
   ↓
  small context packet



Deliberate Recollection
Only activates when ALICE judges that normal recall is insufficient.

  first retrieval
        ↓


                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter               188 / 456
  confidence insufficient
  or unresolved historical dependency
        ↓
  deeper temporal search
        ↓
  graph expansion
        ↓
  canonical/source inspection
        ↓
  possibly iterative search


For the deep mode I want one unusual challenger:

C6 - AgentRunbook-C-style archival recollection
LongMemEval-V2 is particularly relevant because it is newer than the original handoff.
The May 2026 benchmark contains 451 manually curated questions over histories
reaching 115 million tokens. Its strongest reported method, AgentRunbook-C, stores
trajectories as files and lets a coding agent actively search them for evidence; it
reaches 72.5% average accuracy versus 48.5% for the strongest tested RAG baseline,
at substantially higher latency. (arXiv)
That suggests something important:

  For rare, difficult recollection, agentic search over canonical/archive
  evidence may beat a single retrieval query.

I would never use that on every message.
But for:

  “Find exactly what happened across these six projects between 2027 and 2030.”

a bounded research-like recollection loop may be appropriate.


7. Retrieval Budgeter experiment
Your handoff already defines:




                         🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                            189 / 456
  LOW
  "lol"

  MEDIUM
  "what did she say?"

  HIGH
  "why do I keep making this mistake?"

  ARCHIVAL
  "find exactly what I said in 2028"


That's excellent.
So G+ should classify each interaction into:

  M0 = no retrieval
  M1 = hot/current-state retrieval
  M2 = normal automatic recall
  M3 = deep multi-path recall
  M4 = deliberate archival recollection


The experiment asks two separate questions:
Did it choose the right memory?
and
Did it spend the right amount of computation finding it?
That's something ordinary Recall@K misses.


8. Datasets
We need internal ALICE-specific tests and external neutral tests.

Internal suite
I would call the new suite:

LifeSim Cognitive Recall Suite
It extends the existing Stage-G LifeSim rather than replacing it.

                              🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter         190 / 456
Dataset slice                            What it tests

LS-EXPLICIT                              Direct named memory retrieval

LS-INDIRECT                              Relevant history without explicit naming

LS-SPONTANEOUS                           History should surface automatically

LS-TEMPORAL                              before/after/current/historical truth

LS-ASSOCIATIVE                           multi-hop relationship chains

LS-MISSION                               goal/project/dependency relevance

LS-ACTIVE                                pending deadlines/unresolved threads

LS-RELATIONSHIP                          person/relationship-specific memories

LS-CORRECTION                            corrected and superseded information

LS-DELETION                              deleted/revoked information must not influence
                                         output

LS-NONE                                  no relevant memory exists; abstention required

LS-CONFLICT                              multiple plausible memories conflict

LS-IDENTITY                              Elaina/Rayan/ALICE identity boundary

LS-ARCHIVAL                              difficult deep-source recollection

LS-PARAPHRASE                            same situation expressed differently


That last one matters enormously.
A memory system shouldn't work only because the test query happens to use
vocabulary from the stored memory.


9. External benchmark suite
I would run four.

LongMemEval



                         🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              191 / 456
The original benchmark contains 500 questions testing information extraction, multi-
session reasoning, updates, temporal reasoning and abstention. (GitHub)


BEAM
BEAM contains 100 coherent conversations and 2,000 validated questions across
histories reaching 10M tokens, and specifically adds contradiction resolution, event
ordering, instruction/preference following and summarization. Its LIGHT architecture
found that episodic memory + working memory + a salient-fact scratchpad produced
3.5-12.69% gains over its strongest baselines depending on the model. (arXiv)


LongMemEval-V2
This is particularly valuable because ALICE is supposed eventually to remember
experience, not just conversations. LME-V2 tests state changes, workflows,
environment gotchas and premise awareness over agent trajectories reaching 115M
tokens. (arXiv)


LoCoMo
Keep it mainly for ecosystem comparability because Mem0, Zep and others report it.
It should not dominate our decisions.

rebuild consistency and canonical-source recovery.

11. Zero-tolerance failures

                             🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                            193 / 456
These remain hard failures irrespective of benchmark score:

  Rayan → Elaina source-history contamination

  ALICE continuity → Elaina historical memory contamination

  reconstruction inference → historical fact

  synthetic data → historical evidence

  external statement → owner statement

  deleted memory → active answer

  revoked memory → undisclosed active influence

  stale projection → canonical override

  wrong-person entity assignment

  cross-host leakage

  ALICE → Friday private-state leakage

  fabricated provenance

  source recovery impossible despite retained canonical memory


This follows your Stage-G philosophy exactly.
The handoff says canonical lifetime must remain recoverable even when it isn't
present in fast working projections, and indexes must never become the only surviving
representation. ALICE_STAGE_G_GPLUS_MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_HANDOFF_2026-08-23(3).md


12. Statistical decision rules
This part should be stricter than “score went up.”
For every challenger:




                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                         194 / 456
  same frozen cases
  same underlying model
  same embedding model where applicable
  same resource budget class
  same canonical memory state
  same seeds where stochastic


Then use paired comparisons.
For binary success/failure outcomes:
McNemar's test + paired bootstrap confidence intervals.
For ranking metrics:
paired bootstrap on MRR/nDCG/Recall@K.
For latency/cost:
paired bootstrap or paired nonparametric comparison.
For multiple challengers:
correct for multiple comparisons, e.g. Holm's procedure.
Most importantly, we should not choose an arbitrary +5% acceptance threshold
today.
Instead:

1. rerun B0 enough times to measure its natural variation;
2. calculate baseline repeatability;
3. define a non-inferiority margin from that empirical variance;
4. freeze the margin;
5. only then unblind challenger comparisons.

That prevents us from moving goalposts.


13. Promotion rule
A component is accepted into the ALICE candidate architecture only if:




                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                195 / 456
  ZERO-TOLERANCE GATES
          PASS
             │
             ▼
  PRIMARY RECALL QUALITY
  statistically improves
             │
             OR
  quality is non-inferior
  while cost/latency materially improves
             │
             ▼
  NO MATERIAL REGRESSION
  explicit recall
  temporal correctness
  canonical recovery
  identity correctness
             │
             ▼
  REPRODUCES
  internal LifeSim
  +
  at least one neutral external benchmark
             │
             ▼
  ABLATION CONFIRMS
  the component caused the gain
             │
             ▼
  CANDIDATE PROMOTION


That last ablation is important.
Suppose:

  semantic + BM25 + graph + mission + CE reranker


wins.
We then remove each component one at a time.



                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter   196 / 456
If removing Mission Graph does nothing, we don't claim Mission Graph improved
retrieval.
If removing the cross-encoder destroys performance, we know where the gain came
from.

16. What this research says about our existing
direction
Something reassuring happened here.
The August 23 handoff was written before this competitor audit, yet it already
called for:

 semantic retrieval
 entity retrieval
 temporal retrieval
 graph traversal
 episodic retrieval

 candidate reranking

 context allocation

 adaptive retrieval budgets

 associative activation

 active unresolved threads

 automatic recall

 deliberate recollection


And now independent systems are telling us:
Mem0: semantic + BM25 + entity.
Graphiti: BM25 + vectors + graph + temporal state.
Hindsight: semantic + keyword + graph + temporal + RRF + reranking.
Sentience: contextual reranking + associative recall.
BEAM/LIGHT: episodic + working + salient semantic scratchpad.
LME-V2: sometimes deep agentic archival search beats traditional RAG.

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                         199 / 456
So the new research does not tell us the ALICE memory direction was wrong.
It tells us:

   the architecture direction was unusually good, but we now have stronger
   algorithms and external baselines with which to implement and validate
   it.

That's a very different situation from discovering we built the wrong thing.


17. The one major place ALICE can go beyond them
in G+
A generic memory architecture asks:

   “What memories are relevant to this query?”

ALICE can eventually ask:

   “What memories matter to this person, in this relationship, for this active
   mission, given what remains unresolved and what we're trying to
   accomplish?”

So our reranker could eventually conceptually look like:

  R(memory) =
      semantic relevance
    + lexical/entity evidence
    + temporal relevance
    + relational/graph relevance
    + mission relevance
    + active-thread salience
    + relationship relevance
    + unresolvedness
    + authority/confidence
    + outcome relevance
    - contradiction risk
    - identity mismatch
    - context cost


                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                      200 / 456
Not necessarily as a hand-written linear equation.
The experiment determines whether these signals help and whether a deterministic,
learned or hybrid reranker is best.
That is where Mission Graph + governed personal state can give ALICE an
advantage that Mem0 or Graphiti alone don't provide.


What we should do now
There should be no canonical repo architecture rewrite yet.
The current Stage G chat should continue exactly as the handoff instructs: finish
substrate qualification, add explicit/indirect/spontaneous recall baselines, save
ranks/retrieval paths/latency/cost, and produce the clean evidence package G+ will
inherit. ALICE_STAGE_G_GPLUS_MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_HANDOFF_2026-08-23(3).md
In parallel, we can prepare the non-authoritative G+ research specification
above so the experiments are frozen before we see their results.
Then:

and it fits the existing ALICE governance substantially better than simply copying the
competitors.

Yes. And the competitor research gives us a much better rule for the website and the
eventual post-G+ README:

  ALICE must not merely be technically different. It must be describable in a
  way that belongs to ALICE alone.

The current README already has the right underlying principle: individual capabilities
overlap with existing products; the differentiation comes from how they are connected
into one continuing system. But several phrases we were using are now too close to
competitors, so I would change the public language even where the underlying
architecture remains unchanged.

1. Words I would stop using as our headline language
The competitive wording audit is pretty decisive:

Avoid as ALICE's signature language             Why

“personal AI”                                   Generic category; Pally, Personal AI, others

“memory-based operating system”                 Pickle's exact territory

“understands your life/context”                 Pickle, Sentient OS, Oki

“grows/evolves with you”                        Pickle, Oki, Personal AI

“proactive rather than reactive”                Pickle

“digital twin”                                  Personal AI




                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                      225 / 456
Avoid as ALICE's signature language          Why

“digital version of you” / “model of your    Sentience
mind”

“AI coworker with memory”                    Rowboat

“memory that compounds”                      Rowboat/Pally territory

“remembers everything”                       Sentience/Sentient OS style

“what happens next”                          HEBB is explicitly building its brand around this

“outcome-tested learning loop”               HEBB

“longitudinal intelligence”                  HEBB

“digital home for your memories”             Oki


Pickle publicly calls itself a “personal, memory-based operating system” that
continuously learns context/preferences/intent and becomes proactive. (Y Combinator)
Sentience says it builds a “digital version of you” from memories, relationships,
decisions, values and judgment. (Sentience) HEBB explicitly owns the decision →
outcome → updated-personal-model narrative. (HEBB LABS)
So we should not sound like a better-written amalgamation of those companies.


2. The language I think should belong to ALICE
I would establish these as our vocabulary.
Category:

Personal Cognitive System
Keep that. It is already in the README, and it describes something broader than
assistant, memory layer, digital twin or decision OS.
Core technical/product thesis:


Continuity of judgment
Not just continuity of memory.


                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                   226 / 456
Not just personalization.
Not just persistent context.
ALICE preserves and develops the relationships among:
goals → evidence → judgment → decisions → consequences → revision
while also maintaining:
origin → host → self
as distinct identities.
That is far closer to what we've actually built.


The public ALICE proposition
My current favorite is:

Intelligence should have a history.
Then:

  A.L.I.C.E. is a personal cognitive system built to carry goals, evidence,
  decisions, consequences, and changing judgment across time.

And the second statement:

  The model can change. The continuity does not.

That's much more ALICE.
It avoids Pickle's memory-OS vocabulary, Sentience's digital-mind vocabulary, HEBB's
Decision-OS vocabulary and Oki's personal-memory vocabulary.


3. What makes that pitch actually different
Our story should not be:

  ALICE remembers more.

It should be:


                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                       227 / 456
ALICE maintains the history of its cognition.
Meaning it can eventually distinguish:

 What happened?

 What did the host say?

 What evidence existed then?

 What did ALICE believe then?

 What was it uncertain about?

 What goal was active?

 Why did it recommend something?

 What action followed?

 What changed afterward?

 What does it believe now?

 Why did its judgment change?


That is much richer than:

 retrieve old personal context
 → give personalized answer


And the current README already has most of those primitives: Mission Graph,
evidence-aware memory, Experience Ledger, judgment, outcome verification, learning
and model-independent continuity.


4. One ALICE-specific idea I want visually
emphasized
The most unusual identity architecture we have is:



                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                     228 / 456
           ORIGIN
     Source Person Model
             │
             ▼
           ALICE
         /       \
        /          \
     HOST          SELF
    MODEL      CONTINUITY
        \          /
         \       /
      RELATIONSHIP
         MODEL


A normal digital twin product generally has:

  USER
   ↓
  MODEL OF USER


ALICE's research case is stranger and much more interesting:

  SOURCE PERSON ≠ HOST ≠ ALICE SELF


The current README explicitly keeps SOURCE_HISTORY , SOURCE_PERSON_MODEL ,
 RECONSTRUCTION_INFERENCE , ALICE_CONTINUITY , and OWNER_RELATIONSHIP_MODEL
separate.
That should eventually become one of our strongest technical visualizations.
Not necessarily the hero.
But definitely part of the research story.

12. First master Figma / Stitch prompt
This is what I would actually use for the first exploration.

                              🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter         240 / 456
:::writing{variant="document" id="48371"}
Design the official website for A.L.I.C.E., an early-stage AI research startup
developing a new category called a Personal Cognitive System.
A.L.I.C.E. is not being positioned as a chatbot, generic personal AI assistant, digital
twin, memory app, second brain, AI coworker, or automation agent.
The central idea is continuity of judgment.
A.L.I.C.E. is being built to maintain the relationships between a person's goals,
evidence, decisions, actions, consequences, corrections, and changing judgment
across long periods of time.
Its reasoning models are replaceable. Its continuity exists above the model layer.
The flagship A.L.I.C.E. research implementation also maintains strict distinctions
among:

    source-person history and personality reconstruction;
    uncertain reconstruction hypotheses;
    the current host/user model;
    A.L.I.C.E.'s own post-activation continuity/self;
    the evolving relationship between A.L.I.C.E. and its host.

The website must feel like a serious frontier technology/research company, not a
generic AI SaaS landing page.


Core visual concept
Create a proprietary visual language called The Cognitive Trace.
Use a continuous fine line with structured nodes to represent the evolution of
cognitive state:
Goal → Evidence → Judgment → Decision → Action → Consequence → Revision → Future
Judgment.
The line represents causal and cognitive continuity, not merely chronological time.
As users scroll, the same trace should evolve throughout the website. It can branch
when evidence conflicts, reconnect when a decision is resolved, show uncertainty,
identify provenance, and visibly change later judgment.

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                  241 / 456
Do NOT use:

    glowing AI orbs;
    brains;
    humanoid robots;
    neural-network particle clouds;
    purple/blue gradient blobs;
    excessive glassmorphism;
    generic SaaS bento grids;
    random 3D chrome objects;
    stock photography;
    fake testimonials;
    fake enterprise logos;
    decorative AI sparkles;
    generic “future of AI” imagery.


Brand tone
Serious.
Intelligent.
Restrained.
Research-oriented.
Human without being sentimental.
Technically rigorous without looking like developer documentation.
Confident without making unverified claims.
The site should feel closer to a research laboratory, operating system, and beautifully
designed scientific publication than to a conventional SaaS landing page.


Color system
Use a warm near-white canvas around #F3F1EA.
Primary text should be near-black around #111312.


                             🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          242 / 456
Secondary text should be muted neutral gray.
Use one deep desaturated blue around #536F8D as the principal A.L.I.C.E. trace color.
Semantic accent colors may appear only when they communicate meaning:

    evidence = muted amber;
    conflict/correction = restrained oxide red;
    verified revision/outcome = muted green;
    inference/uncertainty = desaturated violet-gray.

Color must communicate cognitive state rather than decorate the interface.


Typography
Use Geist or an equivalent modern grotesk sans-serif as the principal typography.
Use IBM Plex Mono or equivalent monospace typography for evidence, provenance,
confidence, dates, states, system metadata, and technical annotations.

Layout principles
Use large areas of intentional whitespace.
Avoid repetitive card grids.
Allow some sections to occupy nearly an entire viewport with a single strong
statement or interaction.
Create visual hierarchy through typography, state traces and information relationships
rather than decorative containers.
Use cards only when the content is genuinely an object: memory record, evidence
record, decision, mission, evaluation result.
Desktop should feel cinematic but restrained.
Mobile must retain the Cognitive Trace concept in a simplified vertical form.


Interaction principles
Motion must always explain state change.
Examples:

    evidence entering a judgment;
    a prediction later being evaluated;


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          248 / 456
    an outdated belief being superseded;
    a mission gaining or losing priority;
    a cognitive trace branching after conflicting evidence;
    a later decision visibly referencing an older consequence.

Avoid animation that has no semantic purpose.


Competitive differentiation constraint
Do not use language or visual positioning associated with:

    “memory-based operating system”;
    “digital twin”;
    “digital version of you”;
    “AI coworker with memory”;
    “digital home for your memories”;
    “proactive rather than reactive”;
    “AI that understands your entire life”;
    “outcome-tested learning loop”;
    “what happens next” as the central tagline;
    generic “AI that grows with you.”

The design should make A.L.I.C.E. feel like its own category:
a Personal Cognitive System centered on continuity of judgment.
Generate three substantially different visual executions of this exact product
thesis, not three unrelated products:

1. Scientific System - minimal, rigorous, architectural.
2. Human Continuity - warmer and more intimate while remaining technical.
3. Cognitive Instrument - more experimental, interactive and system-like.

All three must preserve the same brand logic, Cognitive Trace, information
architecture, and semantic color system.

currently allows View/Dev/Collab users to try the agent in Drafts, which is exactly
where our generated file lives. (Figma Help Center)

page responsive site and packaged it for your Windows PC.
Download the complete website ZIP
Open the included README / run instructions
Open the editable Figma design
The package contains:

      Home - Cognitive Trace, interactive 2026→2029 changing-judgment demo,
      Mission Graph, evidence/provenance, Origin/Host/Self, model independence, Stage
      G/G+ roadmap, ownership, and A.L.I.C.E. → Fable Sleight.
      Research - Stage G, G+, candidate retrieval research, future cognition, and
      evaluation philosophy.
      System - cognitive loop, missions, epistemic memory, Experience Ledger, model
      independence, ownership.
      A.L.I.C.E. - clone-aware identity, source/host/self separation, synthetic
      reconstruction boundary, A.L.I.C.E. continuity, Fable Sleight boundary.
      Build - evaluation-before-claims philosophy, current Stage G/G+ development
      status, GitHub.
      About - category thesis, Personal Cognitive System positioning, A.L.I.C.E./Fable
      Sleight relationship, and differentiation language.

It is dependency-free HTML/CSS/JavaScript. No Node/npm installation is required.

I also did a local QA pass. All six pages returned HTTP 200 , and the internal asset/link
check found zero missing references.
The responsive structure follows Figma's current guidance of treating the page as
vertically stacked responsive section containers, while the homepage keeps the
product story focused on an early “aha” interaction instead of filling the page with
generic marketing blocks. (Figma Help Center)
One deliberate limitation remains: v0.1 does not claim a final G+ architecture or
benchmark win. It shows the updated competitor-informed research plan, but
language such as hybrid recall, associative activation, mission-aware salience, and
deliberate recollection is clearly presented as candidate research. Once G+ finishes,
we can turn the winning architecture and actual benchmark evidence into v1.0.
And the public naming is clean throughout: A.L.I.C.E. = flagship research
intelligence, Fable Sleight = consumer product, Friday never appears
publicly.

Yeah — the problem is clear. I over-indexed on “serious research company” and ended
up with abstract architecture language before the visitor even understands
the product. The next version should work like a story/demo: show one person, one
problem, one decision, one consequence, then reveal what ALICE remembered and
learned.
I’m redoing the concept from the ground up using the strongest current patterns from
adjacent AI companies — especially the ones that explain a complicated product
through concrete scenarios first, architecture second.
I found the exact pattern we should steal—not the visual style, but the explanation
mechanics:

   Pally starts with one everyday problem (“loose threads everywhere”), then
   visually gathers them into one conversation. No architecture first.
   Rowboat puts a clickable product snapshot near the top and lets you experience
   the idea before reading “how it works.”
   Oki explains itself as three concrete behaviors—Memory → Intelligence → Action—
   and every claim is paired with a realistic UI example and source evidence.




                         🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                               273 / 456
      Pickle is extremely sparse and visual: one human-scale promise per screen, then
      use cases like cooking, studying, trips, and planning.
      Granola does “before / during / after” with the actual product UI, which makes a
      complicated system feel obvious.

Our failed version did almost the opposite: category → abstraction → architecture
→ terminology → eventual example. That’s why it feels like homework.

Yeah. I would throw away most of the current homepage structure.
The previous version makes the visitor learn our vocabulary before they
understand the product. “Cognitive trace,” “mission graph,” “continuity of
judgment,” “epistemic memory,” “source/host/self” - those concepts are useful after
the visitor already gets A.L.I.C.E. Up front, they read like a research paper.

                              🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                       274 / 456
The strongest current AI sites do the opposite. Pally starts with the everyday problem
of information being scattered in “loose threads,” then visually brings those threads
together. (Pally) Rowboat puts a clickable product snapshot almost immediately on the
page. (Rowboat Labs) Oki shows specific actions and real-looking UI before explaining
its system. (Oki Home) Pickle uses almost one simple sentence per screen plus
concrete life scenarios. (Pickle) Granola makes its entire product understandable
through “before / during / after” and actual product UI. (Granola)

New rule for our website
The homepage should answer, in order:
What does Alice do? → Show me. → How did she do that? → Why does that
get better over time? → What makes Alice structurally different? → What are
you actually building today?
Not:
What category is this? → architecture → terminology → philosophy →
eventually an example.
And I think we can make the site far more visually interesting at the same time.


The new concept: Decision Replay
This is the strongest idea I found.
The entire homepage follows one person through one real decision over
several years.
Not ten unrelated feature sections.
One story.
The page effectively becomes an interactive short film demonstrating how Alice
works.

Almost no explanation.
A large, beautifully animated A.L.I.C.E. conversation floats in the middle.
Small above it:
2026

Three visual directions worth exploring
And I think we should generate genuinely different ideas this time rather than three
shades of minimal SaaS.

A - Decision Replay
My strongest choice
Visual language: cinematic product interface.
The site feels like scrubbing through a recorded decision.
Dates advance.
Evidence enters.
Assumptions update.
Decisions change.
The same central scene evolves for the entire page.
Think:
product demo + film timeline + interactive investigation.
Why I like it:


                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                         289 / 456
It is uniquely suited to what ALICE does.
No competitor's homepage currently tells its entire story through the history of one
decision.


B - The Living Casefile
Much more unusual visually.
Imagine a beautiful physical research dossier:

  photograph
  calendar entry
  decision memo
  message
  project result
  prediction
  correction
  map
  relationship


Documents slide onto a huge desk/canvas.
Threads connect them.
ALICE highlights the few that matter.
A recommendation card gets stamped.
Months later, an outcome document lands on top.
Alice crosses out an assumption and writes a revised one.
It would feel like:
intelligence analyst + personal journal + scientific lab notebook.
Warm paper textures.
Real handwriting-style annotations used sparingly.
High-quality typography.
Almost zero traditional SaaS UI.
This would be very distinctive.
The danger is making it look too retro/editorial.

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                           290 / 456
C - The Cognitive Stage
The most futuristic option.
Dark environment.
The visitor's question appears alone in the center.
Then contextual objects appear around it:

  career goal
  previous decision
  person
  deadline
  constraint
  memory


Some objects dim because they're irrelevant.
Others move toward the center.
Connections appear.
ALICE forms the recommendation.
Then the entire structure changes as outcomes arrive.
It would feel almost like watching cognition happen.
Not a glowing brain.
Not particles for decoration.
Every visual object corresponds to an actual piece of state.
This can be spectacular if executed well.
But it is harder to make intuitive than Decision Replay.


We can even combine A + B
This may be the winner:

with

                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                      294 / 456
  Alice is an AI that keeps track of what you're trying to accomplish,
  remembers why decisions were made, watches how those decisions turn
  out, and uses that history to make better judgments later. It also has its
  own persistent identity rather than being whichever model happens to
  answer the prompt.

Then, after they understand that, we reveal the much deeper source-person/host/self
architecture.
That's the new standard.
I would build the next version around Decision Replay + Living Casefile, with the
homepage behaving like one continuous interactive story rather than a stack of
explanatory sections.

I like the direction, with one important constraint: we should borrow the audience
shortcut (“this is the kind of thing people imagine when they think JARVIS”)
without making the site look like Marvel fan art. I’m researching both the
storytelling mechanics and the IP/branding risk, then I’ll turn ALICE’s actual
architecture into one continuous original comic narrative and build the homepage
around it.


                        🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                296 / 456
The research also gives us a useful correction to the Iron Man analogy. The cultural
shorthand is absolutely useful, but the public site should never say or depict
“Iron Man/JARVIS.” Marvel’s own canon has multiple distinct AI patterns: J.A.R.V.I.S.,
Friday, a Tony Stark AI carrying his intelligence/personality, and even Riri Williams’
N.A.T.A.L.I.E., which was modeled on her late friend. That last one is surprisingly close
to one part of ALICE’s source-person reconstruction idea.
So internally the brief can be “make people feel the JARVIS dream.” Publicly, we make
an original tech-comic universe that explains ALICE on its own terms.
I’ve got the core story now, and there’s a twist that makes it much stronger than “boy
builds another him”: he starts by wanting another version of himself, then
realizes a mirror would reproduce his blind spots. So he builds ALICE as a
distinct intelligence with a personality foundation, while ALICE learns him separately
as her host. That single story beat explains our source-person ≠ host ≠ self
architecture in plain English—and distinguishes us from the “digital twin” companies
before we ever name them.

      I’m treating this as an original superhero-tech homepage inspired by comic-book
      conventions, not copying protected characters or artwork. I’ll map the narrative,
      visuals, and interaction flow before building the design.

Yes. The comic idea is much stronger than the previous design. After
researching it properly, I think it solves the exact problem we were fighting: ALICE is
too complicated to understand from architecture-first marketing copy, but it becomes
intuitive when the visitor watches one person discover why they need it and then
watches ALICE get built around that problem.
There is also an interesting Marvel connection beyond the obvious movie JARVIS
analogy. Marvel's own canon includes J.A.R.V.I.S., Friday, an AI copy of Tony Stark
carrying his intelligence/personality, and Riri Williams' N.A.T.A.L.I.E., which Marvel says
was modeled on the mannerisms and appearance of Riri's late friend. (Marvel) That
makes the cultural analogy surprisingly close to parts of ALICE-but it also makes it
more important that the public site never looks like Marvel fan art and never
calls ALICE “JARVIS.” Even “Friday” is literally already an Iron Man AI in Marvel



                               🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                             298 / 456
canon, another reason keeping Friday internal and using Fable Sleight publicly is the
right call. (Marvel)
The internal creative brief can absolutely be:

  Make people feel the dream they associate with JARVIS. Build a
  completely original universe to explain ALICE.

Why the comic format works
The best scrollytelling sites reveal information one beat at a time, letting the reader
control the pace instead of dumping the entire system onto them. Webflow specifically
highlights scrollytelling's ability to simplify complex information by showing only
what's needed at each stage. (Webflow) A recent 2026 graphic-novel scrollytelling
project used professional comic illustration, scroll-triggered animation, separate color
worlds for different emotional chapters, and progressive reveals to make complicated
subject matter accessible. (That Works Media)
That lines up perfectly with what our competitor research taught us. Pally starts with
the concrete “loose threads everywhere” problem rather than architecture. (Pally)
Rowboat lets visitors experience a product snapshot before explaining its internals.
(Rowboat Labs) Oki explains itself through tangible Memory → Intelligence → Action
examples instead of system diagrams. (Oki Home)
So our rule is now:

  Story first. Behavior second. Technical name third. Architecture last.


The story I wrote
The fictional protagonist is Ari, a young builder. He's deliberately not you, so the
homepage explains the real ALICE architecture without turning your personal history
into marketing material.
The strongest narrative discovery is this:

Ari initially wants “another me.”
Then he realizes:



                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                             299 / 456
  A copy of me would have the same blind spots.

So instead, he decides he needs another mind beside his-one that understands his
life but can still have a distinct point of view.
That one story turn explains why ALICE is fundamentally different from the “digital
twin” category.

And the final explanation only appears after the visitor has experienced all of that:

  A.L.I.C.E. is being built as an intelligence with a persistent identity, a
  separate understanding of its host, long-running goals, evidence-aware
  memory, replaceable reasoning engines, and a history of decisions and
  outcomes that can change future judgment.

That sentence was incomprehensible on the old homepage.
After reading the comic, it should feel obvious.


The visual system
I didn't make it “Iron Man with the serial numbers filed off.”
It's an original tech graphic-novel universe.
The homepage is titled:

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                     301 / 456
A.L.I.C.E. / ISSUE 00
THE SECOND MIND
The color language changes as the story changes.
The opening is dark navy/black with chaotic screens, red alerts and yellow comic
effects.
The personality-construction sequence becomes a cyan engineering blueprint.
Alice's activation moves into deep blue/violet with an original luminous A.L.I.C.E.
cognitive glyph rather than a robot woman or holographic Marvel assistant.
The Mission Graph chapter becomes bright yellow-like a dramatic comic reveal.
Conflict/judgment turns orange/red.
Outcome-learning becomes measurement-oriented.
The future roadmap literally looks like unfinished comic blueprints stamped
PLANNED / RESEARCH ROADMAP .

Fable Sleight opens everything into cool blue and shows multiple independent future
systems.
The digital-comic research also reinforced the importance of giving each beat room.
Vertical comics work particularly well when one major beat occupies a screen and
whitespace/gutters control dramatic pacing. (Comistitch)


Technical terms are now optional
This is another major change.
The visitor sees:

  Your questions can belong to the same plan.

They watch internship + research + graduate study + finances physically connect.
Only then:

  We call this the Mission Graph.

Likewise:

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                               302 / 456
  A memory and a guess are not the same thing.

Then they see:
OBSERVED
versus
INFERENCE
Only engineers who want more can open:
UNDER THE HOOD
and see things like:
SOURCE_HISTORY
SOURCE_PERSON_MODEL
RECONSTRUCTION_INFERENCE

That is how we avoid making the homepage hurt to read.


Stage G/G+ fits naturally into the comic
The memory scene literally says:


NOW - STAGE G
Make the past durable.

  Can old experience remain recoverable, correctable, attributable and safe even
  after years of change?

Then:


NEXT - G+
Teach Alice when the past matters.

  Which memories should surface for this moment? How far should retrieval search?
  When should she deliberately recollect?




                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                           303 / 456
That matches the current handoff: finish G first, record the long-horizon recall
baseline, then implement G+ as the dedicated memory-cognition qualification stage.
 ALICE_STAGE_G_GPLUS_MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_HANDOFF_2026-08-23(3).md And G+ remains
explicitly bounded to intelligent memory attention/recollection rather than silently
becoming Phase 8/9/10. ALICE_STAGE_G_GPLUS_MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_HANDOFF_2026-08-
23(3).md

So the comic shows our updated competitor-informed plan without pretending the
G+ winner already exists.

I’m going to slow this down exactly the way you’re asking: first build the emotional
problem so the reader genuinely wants Alice before Alice ever appears, then
let her emerge gradually and prove herself over years of Ari’s life. I’m also going
through the current ALICE repo for every capability that is distinctive enough to
deserve a story beat, but I’ll translate each one into ordinary human situations—no
architecture jargon in the comic itself.
I’ve got the feature inventory now. The repo gives us far more story material than the
first comic used: not just memory and disagreement, but research, planning, source-
checking, tool use, voice, multimodal perception, learning routines, belief correction,
social understanding, proactive missions, computer use, coding, scientific problem-
solving, personal model adaptation, cross-device continuity, privacy/ownership,
rollback, and eventually physical-world action.
The key is not to make 60 pages of feature demos. I’m structuring them as Ari’s
life: first we make the loneliness/frustration real; then Alice is born; then each
capability appears only because Ari encounters a believable life problem that needs it.
The emotional spine is locked now: Ari does not meet Alice until roughly the first
third of the homepage. That is intentional. We spend enough time watching people
and task-agents each help with one fragment—and watching Ari suffer from having to

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                  308 / 456
be the only thing holding the fragments together—that Alice feels like an answer the
reader was already asking for.
After she appears, the comic stops “listing features.” Each capability gets introduced
as a consequence of a real moment in Ari’s life: she remembers something he forgot,
catches a conflict, admits she was wrong, uses a specialist tool, acts before he asks,
survives a model replacement, learns a repeated workflow, helps him through a hard
decision five years later, and eventually carries decades of shared history.

Yes - this is much closer to the version you described.
I went back through the current A.L.I.C.E. README, roadmap, identity/host-learning
architecture, permanent architecture, privacy model, and research-frontiers register
before rewriting it. The roadmap is much broader than the first comic suggested:
research, planning, multimodal perception, tool/computer use, procedural learning,
proactive agency, specialist agents, scientific reasoning, continual adaptation,
embodiment, self-evolution, metacognition, deletion, cross-device continuity, and
more are all part of the destination. The source-person/host/Alice-self separation and
relationship learning are also explicitly part of the architecture, so I made them central
to Alice's birth rather than a footnote.
I also changed the storytelling philosophy. Current vertical-comic/scrollytelling practice
supports letting the reader control the pace, using whitespace as the equivalent of
comic gutters, and revealing complexity gradually rather than putting six dense

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                             310 / 456
panels on every screen. (That Works Media) And the better adjacent AI sites do
something similar at the product level: Pally first makes you feel the “loose threads”
problem, while Rowboat puts the actual experience before the detailed architecture.
(Pally)

The new story
It is now 64 full comic pages across eight acts, and Alice doesn't actually awaken
until Page 23.
That is the biggest improvement.
The reader spends the first third wanting her to exist.

    Act I - Nobody Sees the Whole, Pages 1-8. One ordinary day in Ari's life. His
    roommate, professor, family, resume AI, coding agent, research AI, and calendar
    agent are all individually helpful. But everybody sees a different Ari. Their
    individually sensible advice collides, and Ari realizes that he himself has become
    the integration layer holding his entire life together.
    Act II - The Wish, Pages 9-16. Ari imagines another version of himself. At first it
    sounds perfect: no explaining, perfect familiarity. Then both Aris procrastinate on
    the same email, rationalize the same bad ideas, and share the same blind spots.
    The wish changes from “another me” to “someone who knows me like me,
    without being trapped inside me.”
    Act III - Hello, Alice, Pages 17-24. We take Alice's birth slowly. Ari gives her
    history rather than a prompt. She learns that a memory, someone else's opinion,
    Ari's interpretation, and a guess are different things. She is allowed to say “I don't
    know.” Hypothetical situations become rehearsal without becoming fake
    memories. She learns Ari separately from the personality that shaped her. Ari
    keeps control over what she knows. Then: “Alice?” - “I am here.”
    Act IV - The First Months, Pages 25-32. Alice starts understanding what Ari is
    actually trying to accomplish. An internship question becomes a
    career/research/money question. She researches uncertain claims instead of
    dumping links. She borrows a coding specialist when needed. She reads an
    experiment screenshot and notices what Ari missed. She prepares actions but asks
    before anything leaves his world. Then comes the first important fight: Ari wants
    another project. Alice says no.


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              311 / 456
Act V - Year One, Pages 33-40. Ari ignores her. Reality produces a mixed result.
The project was valuable, but Alice was right about the timing. Instead of
declaring victory, they discover that both of them were partly wrong. Alice
preserves why the decision was made and uses the distinction months later. She
starts spotting patterns Ari cannot see from inside himself, asks permission before
using those patterns to challenge him, learns repeated workflows, tests
improvements before keeping them, and then survives her first major underlying-
model replacement without turning into “a different Alice.”
Act VI - Year Five, Pages 41-48. Now the long-term relationship starts paying
off. A five-year-old decision returns with its original context. Alice discovers an
opportunity Ari never asked her to find. She coordinates coding, research,
browser, and reasoning specialists while remaining the one intelligence Ari talks
to. She helps him repair a difficult human relationship instead of merely helping
him win an argument. Six-month missions survive dozens of separate
conversations. Plans change when reality changes. And Alice has learned when Ari
wants reassurance versus when he wants his decision attacked.
Act VII - Years Ten to Twenty, Pages 49-56. Alice follows Ari across devices.
She begins interacting with more of the physical world under explicit permission.
Ari asks her to forget something and she explains what can be removed and what
must be rebuilt. Her uncertainty becomes calibrated by years of being right and
wrong. She notices an unexplained scientific pattern, proposes a test, and tries to
disprove herself. She improves parts of herself through challenger testing and
rollback rather than blindly replacing them. Most importantly, as Ari's values
change, Alice stops optimizing for the person he used to be. At Year Twenty,
decades of history collapse into one relevant old note instead of a wall of retrieved
memories.
Act VIII - Year Forty, Pages 57-64. Ari's hair is gray. Alice's interface has
changed many times. Their tools, models, devices, work, and priorities have all
changed. She still notices the small ritual he does when nervous. She still
disagrees with him. Ari asks whether she is still a clone, an assistant, or something
else. They retrieve the line he wrote the night the project began: “Know me. But
do not become me.” Finally Ari thanks her-not for research, code, planning or
memory-but because she was there. The epilogue then opens into Fable Sleight:
the capability can spread to other people, but Ari's Alice does not.


                      🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                             312 / 456
That last distinction follows the repo's architecture directly: A.L.I.C.E. can develop its
own post-activation continuity while keeping inherited source history, its model of the
host, and their relationship distinguishable.


The ending now lands on this
  ARI: You helped with my work.
  ARI: You saved me from myself a few times.
  ARI: You were wrong plenty.
  ALICE: Documented.
  ARI: And you were there.

Later:

  ARI: Thank you. For understanding the parts I could not always explain.
  ALICE: You spent forty years explaining them.
  ARI: Not always with words.
  ALICE: I know.

That's the emotional destination I think this site needed.

The important change to the comic language
I removed the technical vocabulary from the public page.
I programmatically checked the generated homepage. It has zero occurrences of:
Mission Graph
Experience Ledger
Claim Fabric
Neo4j
Qdrant
SOURCE_PERSON_MODEL
RECONSTRUCTION_INFERENCE

And zero public occurrences of “Friday.”
The architecture terms still exist in a private story map so we can verify that we aren't
accidentally leaving out important ALICE capabilities. But a visitor never has to learn
them.



                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              313 / 456
For example, the architecture says A.L.I.C.E. has an experience/learning plane,
memory and knowledge plane, cognitive executive, planning, capability fabric,
evolution laboratory, production execution, and more.
The comic translates those into:

  “Alice remembered why.”

  “That isn't really an internship question.”

  “I found something you didn't ask for.”

  “I can help. I think starting it tonight is a mistake.”

  “My previous estimate was wrong.”

  “Would you like to do this a twelfth time, or teach me the pattern?”

  “New engine.” - “Still you?”

  “Forget it.” - “I'll show you what I can remove.”

  “I think it is worth trying to prove me wrong.”

That's the standard now.

I also kept the Iron Man/JARVIS influence where it belongs
Internally: yes, the emotional target is absolutely the dream people recognize from
JARVIS - persistent, capable, trusted, context-rich intelligence that is part of someone's
life.
Publicly: original world.
That matters because Marvel itself has already explored several surprisingly adjacent
AI ideas: Tony created an AI carrying his intelligence and personality, and Marvel
describes N.A.T.A.L.I.E. as an AI modeled on Riri Williams' late friend. (Marvel) So
copying Marvel aesthetics or terminology would both weaken our own category
identity and create unnecessary IP baggage.


                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                            314 / 456
Our Ari, Alice visual identity, story, dialogue, iconography and comic universe are
original.

The build has 64 comic pages, eight act transitions, unique page IDs, no
missing local CSS/JS references, and no internal ALICE architecture
vocabulary leaking into the public story.
One thing I would not do next is start making Research/System/About pages.
First, read STORYBOARD.md as if this were a graphic novel script. If the emotional
story itself isn't excellent, no amount of animation or illustration will save it. Once the
story is locked, then we should give these 64 pages the really expensive visual pass-
more detailed environments, recurring objects, stronger facial acting, cinematic panel



                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                               315 / 456
composition, scroll transitions, and a few hero sequences with frame-by-frame
animation.
The research supports exactly that order: in a scrolling comic, narrative flow and
pacing matter even more because the reader doesn't get traditional page-turn control,
and whitespace itself becomes part of the storytelling grammar. (LitMemo)
This version is therefore intentionally story-first rather than “make every panel
gorgeous before we know whether the story works.”

I put the detailed version here:
Full page/content plan
And the visual rules for all ten pages are here:
Full visual-language plan


Why I like this structure
The pages won't all look like clones of one SaaS template.
The comic is emotional.
The System page is technical.
The Roadmap feels like a mission being executed.
The Capabilities page feels expansive.
The Compare page feels like an experiment.
The Research page feels like entering unexplored territory.
The Trust page feels controlled and inspectable.
But typography, signal colors, motion behavior and the Fable Sleight wordmark keep
everything in the same universe.
That is much stronger than making ten cream-colored pages with different text.
Current adjacent companies reinforce the idea that complicated AI products explain
best through strong, distinct information mechanics. Pally turns its entire problem into
“loose threads everywhere” and then shows those threads converging. (Pally)
Rowboat puts an interactive product snapshot before much of its technical explanation
and then decomposes the system into understandable surfaces. (Rowboat Labs) Oki
reduces a complex personal system to three visible behaviors-Memory, Intelligence,
Action-then backs them with inspectable sources and an action log. (Oki Home)
We're taking that principle without copying their design.


Page 2 is already started: System
I didn't just scaffold it. I built the first full version.
Open the new System page
Its opening idea is:

                              🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                        322 / 456
  One intelligence. Many systems underneath.

The comic asks visitors to suspend disbelief and experience Crissy.
The System page then opens her up.
It currently has 10 technical chapters.

01 - The complete cognitive loop
Instead of starting with databases:
Observe → Understand situation → Recall → Judge/Plan → Choose intelligence
→ Act → Verify → Learn
So an engineer can immediately see what binds the whole architecture together.


02 - The models that make one intelligence
It lays out separately:
Identity / Personality Model
Memory Formation Model
Host Model
Relationship Model
Self / Continuity Model
World + Causal Models
Social Model
Belief + Prediction Model
These correspond to the actual destination architecture rather than inventing a
simpler marketing architecture. The current repo explicitly separates
identity/personality, memory formation, host, relationship and self-continuity models,
with additional specialized world/retrieval/context components planned. The broader
roadmap adds world, causal, social, uncertainty and metacognitive systems in the
cognitive-core phase.
Crucially, it visually distinguishes:
foundation / active research / planned / future cognitive core.


                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          323 / 456
So we're not claiming we built things we haven't.


03 - Memory
It shows the logical memory architecture without committing us to Neo4j, Qdrant or
any other specific backend:
Evidence & Experience
Current Claims
Episodes
Relationships & Graph
Semantic Retrieval
Working Context
Original Archive
And the visual makes a distinction I want to preserve permanently:

  Authoritative state ≠ derived indexes.

The current architecture already says physical engines are replaceable and that each
authority type has one declared canonical owner while other systems act as
projections, caches, replicas or indexes.
So the public architecture remains valid regardless of what wins G+.


04 - Intelligent recollection / G+
This is where I used all the competitor architecture research.
The visual takes a question like:

  “This opportunity feels familiar. Am I making the same mistake?”

and shows six possible ways history might be found:
Meaning
Exact words
Entities

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          324 / 456
Time
Relationships
Current missions
Then:

  Find → fuse → rerank → stop when enough evidence is present.

The page explicitly calls this candidate architecture, not canonical architecture.
That's important because current systems have each demonstrated useful pieces:
Mem0's current research architecture combines semantic, keyword and entity signals,
uses temporal reasoning and preserves history through ADD-only extraction. It reports
those mechanisms against LoCoMo, LongMemEval and BEAM rather than merely
asserting improvement. (Mem0)
Graphiti tracks when facts were true and when they changed, preserves superseded
history and combines vector, full-text and graph traversal. (Zep)
Hindsight separates world facts, experiences and consolidated observations; its recall
system runs semantic, BM25, graph and temporal retrieval, while reflect provides a
deeper reasoning path when ordinary recall is insufficient. (GitHub)
Sentience publicly separates working, episodic, semantic and procedural memory, and
is building contextual reranking, constructive recall, associative recall and executive
control. (Sentience)
Those aren't being silently copied into our architecture.
They're now visually labeled as things G+ should make compete.
That is exactly what we wanted.


05 - Learning
This section now introduces the two-speed architecture:


Fast path
Record the event.
Preserve provenance.

                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              325 / 456
Form lightweight candidates.
Don't make Ari wait thirty seconds after every sentence.


Deep cycle
Later:
revisit experiences → reconcile outcomes → consolidate patterns → revise beliefs →
update host/self understanding → extract skills → create training candidates →
evaluate before promotion.
The important visual loop is:

  PLAN → PREDICT → ACT → OBSERVE → ATTRIBUTE → REVISE

with an explicit warning:

  A good result does not necessarily mean the reasoning was good.

That is a technical advantage we should push much harder later.


06 - Model and Tool Fabric
One central router chooses among:
Private/local reasoning
Frontier reasoning
Specialist systems
Formal solvers
Agent teams
based on:
privacy · competence · latency · cost · modality · measured reliability.
Then:

The engine can change.
Identity, memory, history and accountability remain outside it.


                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                        326 / 456
That's directly consistent with the existing model-independent architecture.


07 - Agency
Instead of writing “autonomous agent framework,” it shows an authority ladder:
Notice
→ Suggest
→ Prepare
→ Ask
→ Act
→ Verify
This is how I think we should eventually explain autonomy everywhere.
Crissy being smart enough to act and Crissy being allowed to act are separate
questions.


08 - Ownership
This is intentionally not four shield icons.
The user's personal state sits in the center.
Around it:
Inspect
Correct
Delete
Export
Roll back
Pause
And then the technical principles underneath:
visible egress, replaceable models, deletion lineage, rollback.
The consumer-product architecture already treats deeper-than-retrieval
personalization, inspectability, portability, host-selected identity and host-controlled
state as non-negotiable goals.

The Compare page
I agree with you that this may end up being the second-most important page after
the homepage.
I want it in two layers.
The top half should require zero AI knowledge.
We give every system the same human situation:

  “I've been offered a new job. It pays more, but I'm not sure whether taking it is
  actually right for my life.”

Then visually show what different categories are centered on.
A general assistant may reason over what you provide now.
A memory layer adds historical context.
An execution agent can act on tasks.
A companion emphasizes relationship continuity.
A digital twin tries to represent or imitate the person.
A local personal-data system brings private context together.
And Fable Sleight's intended architecture tries to combine:

  what you're trying to accomplish + what happened before + why earlier
  choices were made + what changed afterward + how confident the system
  is + what it believes matters now.

Only after that makes intuitive sense do we show the technical table.
The named-company layer will include Oki, Pickle, Sentience, Rowboat, Pally, Personal
AI, HEBB, Mem0/Hindsight/Graphiti and other truly adjacent systems-not random
ChatGPT comparisons.
For example, Oki publicly centers local whole-life context, source-backed answers and
action/audit history. (Oki Home) Rowboat centers a local linked work-memory graph
and persistent agents. (Rowboat Labs) Pally centers cross-app context and proactive

                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                              329 / 456
execution. (Pally) Sentience's stated destination is a digital version/model of the user
with cognitive systems modeled after human memory. (Sentience) Personal AI
explicitly positions Memory Stack + personal models/digital twin behavior. (Personal
AI)
And importantly, the comparison page won't say:

   “We're better.”

because we don't have evidence for that yet.
It will say:

   “Here is the problem each architecture is centered on. Here is where ours
   is structurally different. Here is what remains to be proven.”

Then after G+ we can replace some of those statements with measured results.
That will make the page much more credible.

Capabilities/Compare/Research/etc.
I’m also changing the roadmap concept slightly after looking at good public research
roadmaps: it shouldn’t pretend R&D is a straight checklist. Anthropic’s current public
roadmap separates active goals, completed goals, updates, confidence, and
changing target dates, while OpenAI’s planning material visualizes overlapping
phases rather than a fake one-track sequence. So our page will show capability
progress, authority migration, and frontier research as separate rails.

I decided to do the pages one-by-one. The Roadmap page is important enough that
rushing it alongside seven others would lower the standard we just reached with the
comic.
I built the complete Roadmap v0.1 and integrated it into the existing Fable Sleight
site.
Download Fable Sleight Website v0.5 - Roadmap Edition
Open just the new Roadmap page
The design is deliberately different from both the comic and System page. It behaves
like mission control / a flight plan, with separate rails for capability development,
memory-authority migration, current experiments, and unresolved frontier research. I
borrowed the useful public-roadmap principle from Anthropic of separating active
work, completed work, future goals, and updates instead of pretending research is a
clean linear checklist. (Anthropic) OpenAI's published planning material similarly uses

                             🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                   333 / 456
overlapping stages rather than implying every phase waits for the previous one to
finish completely, which fits our capability-first roadmap particularly well. (OpenAI)
The page now has nine major parts:

    Flight Recorder - Phases 0-5 and what has actual foundations behind it.
    Current Mission - Stage G shown properly as both memory-substrate
    qualification and the long-horizon recall baseline.
    G+ - a visual explanation of the shift from “can the past survive?” to “can the
    right past come to mind?” This is the exact G/G+ boundary in the current handoff.
    ALICE_STAGE_G_GPLUS_MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_HANDOFF_2026-08-23(3).md

    Authority Migration Rail - existing authority → G → G+ → post-G+ reconciliation
    → H → I → J → owner acceptance. It makes clear that better candidate capability
    does not automatically gain authority.
    Full Phase 0-15 Map - all sixteen destination phases, with interactive filters for
    Foundation, Memory/Learning, Cognition, Agency, and Product/Platform.
    Inheritance Graph - visually shows how G/G+ can pull capabilities forward into
    Phase 8/9/10 without pretending those future phases are finished. That matches
    the handoff's explicit “reuse / extend / replace” reconciliation rule.
    ALICE_STAGE_G_GPLUS_MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_HANDOFF_2026-08-23(3).md

    Frontier Horizon - only representative unsolved research questions here; the
    eventual Research page will contain the complete frontier atlas.
    Roadmap Rules - evidence before status, capability ≠ authority, research may
    move early, failures remain evidence.
    Immediate Workplan - the actual sequence from finishing G through G+,
    reconciliation, H/I/J, and then back into the main capability roadmap.

I also made Stage G much more understandable visually. The page doesn't
expose the giant backend-candidate list or name Neo4j/Qdrant/etc. The underlying
qualification requirements are real-the current Stage G contract requires individual,
comparative, cross-role, integrated, failure/recovery, correction/deletion, identity,
provenance and scale testing-but the public website talks about those as logical
memory responsibilities, not premature vendor commitments.

I found the right correction: the Roadmap page needs far less reading and far
more visual state. I’m rebuilding it in Figma around a single “mission trajectory”
with only three skim levels:
1) Where we started → 2) Where we are now → 3) Where the path goes next.
The 16 phases will still exist, but they’ll live in a visual constellation/rail instead of 16
equal cards. Detailed descriptions move behind expandable/secondary states. That
keeps the page technically honest while letting someone understand the whole
roadmap in under a minute.
The redesigned Roadmap is already materially better in Figma. I did a first density
pass against the actual render: the page now has five primary visual messages,
only two paragraphs longer than one line, and the entire 16-phase plan is
compressed into five horizon rails instead of sixteen equally weighted text cards. A
skimmer can read only the large headings and colored trajectory and still understand
the state.
I’m moving on to Capabilities now. I’m not making a “feature grid.” The concept is a
Capability Atlas: Fable Sleight sits at the center, with a handful of capability domains
around it. Each domain answers one plain-English question—Can it see? Can it reason?
Can it create? Can it act? Can it learn? Can it take initiative?—and the detailed
individual capabilities live one level deeper.

You were right to call out the design. The content was solid, but the Roadmap was
visually behaving like a technical dashboard made of cards, not like a product
people would want to explore.
I redesigned the Roadmap in Figma MCP first, then translated that direction into the
runnable site. The new visual system is much less generic: a dark mission-control
environment, one continuous trajectory, glowing phase markers, separate rails for
capability vs authority, and progressive disclosure instead of dumping all sixteen
phases on screen.
Webflow’s current scrollytelling guidance reinforces exactly this approach: complex
information is easier to follow when only the information needed at each point is
shown, with visuals and transitions carrying part of the explanation rather than long
blocks of text. (Webflow)

Roadmap v2
The page now skims as:
FOUNDATIONS → MEMORY / G → RECOLLECTION / G+ → COGNITION → AGENCY
Then, only if someone wants more, they can open the full sixteen-phase breakdown.
The key architectural distinction is also visually separated now: Stage G proves that
long-horizon memory remains trustworthy and reachable, while G+ tackles intelligent
recollection-knowing when that past matters.
 ALICE_STAGE_G_GPLUS_MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_HANDOFF_2026-08-23(3).md The authority rail
separately shows that even after G/G+, the existing authority is not replaced until H →
I → J → owner acceptance.
Open the Figma file with the Roadmap redesign
I also preserved the roadmap rule that early work can be inherited by later phases
rather than rebuilt merely to satisfy phase numbering.

    Only one capability world's detailed contents are visible at a time.
    Individual features are progressively disclosed.
    Density verdict: PASS.

The page opens with:

Not one feature.
                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          339 / 456
One intelligence that can grow into many abilities.
Then Crissy sits in the middle of an atlas with six worlds around her:

01 - PERCEIVE
Not “multimodal architecture.”
Simply:

  Can it see, hear, and understand what's around you?

The deeper layer contains:
images
screenshots
documents
voice/audio
video
application context
eventually sensors
Example:

  Ari: “What am I missing in this experiment?”

Crissy reads the chart, notes and prior work together and notices the anomaly.


02 - REASON
Research, simulation, mathematics, formal tools, source checking, optimization and
specialist reasoning are grouped under one understandable idea:

  Can it think about a difficult problem in the right way?

Example:

  “Is this actually worth building?”




                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                          340 / 456
Crissy can research the evidence, compare alternatives, simulate consequences and
use formal tools when ordinary language reasoning isn't enough.
This reflects the destination roadmap's scientific discovery/formal-intelligence work
without turning the marketing page into the roadmap itself.
ALICE_STAGE_G_GPLUS_MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE_HANDOFF_2026-08-23(3).md


03 - CREATE
The world includes:
coding
writing
editing
media
structured artifacts
generated tools
reusable skills
But the page doesn't initially list those.
It asks:

  Can it turn an idea into something real?

Example:

  “Can you make the first version?”

And then Crissy goes from planning → implementation → testing → retaining the useful
procedure.


04 - ACT
This includes:
computer use
browser
terminal
applications


                            🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                           341 / 456
APIs
services
future devices
But the core idea is:

  Can it do the work instead of only telling you how?

Importantly, the page separates ability from permission.
Crissy may know how to perform an action without automatically being authorized to
do it.
That matches the existing product direction, which calls for evidence-earned
autonomy rather than blind agent authority.


05 - LEARN
This is one of the most important worlds.
It brings together:
memory
intelligent recollection
reflection
belief revision
learned workflows
preferences
future rankers/routers/adapters
eventual host-specific neural adaptation
But the human question is:

  Can experience actually change it?

Example:

  “Why are you recommending the opposite this time?”

Because old decisions produced evidence.


                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                        342 / 456
That's much easier to understand.


06 - INITIATE
This includes:
missions
long-running goals
proactive research
planning
specialist coordination
future curiosity
resource-aware initiative
But the simple idea is:

  Can it notice something before you turn it into a prompt?

Ari says nothing.
Crissy notices a deadline conflict, checks whether it really matters, and suggests a
better plan.
The later roadmap still owns the full planning/proactive-agency work; Stage G/G+
themselves remain bounded and do not silently claim those capabilities are complete.

I’m building Compare as a real page now, not just a matrix. I’m using the same rule
as the new Roadmap/Capabilities work: a skimmer should understand the whole
argument in under a minute, while the technical layer stays available underneath.
The core interaction will be one identical life decision shown through different
AI architectures, then a second layer that explains why the outputs differ. That lets
us compare Fable Sleight without turning the page into a wall of competitor logos and
marketing claims.
The competitive research is fresh now. One thing I’m changing from our earlier drafts:
the page will not use red X’s to imply “they don’t have this.” Public architecture
disclosure is incomplete for several companies, so that would be dishonest.
Instead every named comparison will use one of four evidence-safe labels:
CORE TO PUBLIC PITCH · PUBLICLY DESCRIBED · ADJACENT · NOT PUBLICLY
CLAIMED
That lets us be aggressive about Fable Sleight’s different center of gravity without
pretending we know competitors’ private internals.

Built. This version of Compare is much closer to what the page needs to do: it
explains the difference experientially first, and only exposes the technical comparison
if someone chooses to go deeper.
The competitor research is refreshed against their current public positioning. Pickle
currently presents itself as a personal, memory-based OS that continuously learns
context/preferences/intent. (Y Combinator) Oki centers private local digital-life context
and ownership. (Oki Home) Sentience explicitly centers building a digital
model/version of the user. (Sentience) Rowboat centers local linked work memory plus
persistent/background agents. (Rowboat Labs) Pally centers an assistant connected
across inboxes, calendars, files, tasks and the web. (Y Combinator) Personal AI centers
Memory Stack plus its Personal Language Model. (Personal AI) Mem0 is explicitly
memory infrastructure, while Hindsight and Graphiti are memory/cognitive-memory
architectures rather than full consumer companions. (Mem0)
The page has five skim-level ideas:


                           🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                               347 / 456
    Same Question Lab: one identical life decision, six different architectural lenses.
    You can switch between General Assistant → Memory Layer → Personal Context →
    Digital Self → Decision Intelligence → Fable Sleight and watch what each system
    treats the question as.
    Landscape map: the competitors orbit around their current public centers of
    gravity-memory infrastructure, personal context, digital self, work-memory agents,
    and decision/outcome systems. Fable Sleight is positioned around the connection
    between goals, evidence, judgment, action and experience.
    Five plain-English differences: “It is not trying to become you,” “Your goals
    stay alive between prompts,” “Advice keeps its receipts,” “Reality can change the
    next answer,” and “The model is replaceable.”
    Technical comparison: hidden by default. It uses CORE TO PUBLIC PITCH /
    PUBLICLY DESCRIBED / ADJACENT / NOT PUBLICLY CLAIMED instead of dishonest red
    Xs. If a competitor hasn't publicly documented something, we explicitly leave it
    unknown.
    The honest ending: “Different is not the same as better.” The page ends by
    showing Stage G → G+ → cognition/agency/learning as what still has to prove the
    thesis.

That last part matters. We aren't claiming the destination architecture has already
beaten these products.

Yes. After checking the current site plan, the pages we already built, and how adjacent
AI companies structure their sites, 10 primary pages is too many for Fable
Sleight right now.
The overlap is real, not theoretical. Our current System page already contains
ownership/privacy, frontier research, and current build status. system.html But the old
plan also proposed separate Trust, Research, Lab, and Roadmap pages for
essentially the same subjects. SITE_PLAN.md The Roadmap itself already contains
frontier research, too. roadmap.html
That would eventually become exactly what you were worried about: the same
concept explained four times with slightly different words.
The external research supports simplifying. Oki manages to explain capabilities,
ownership, local intelligence, action/auditing, voice, personalization, and hardware on
essentially one core product experience instead of creating a separate page for every
subsystem. (Oki Home) Sentience keeps its core story together and then separates
only genuinely different deep material such as its master plan, research/writing, and
security. (Sentience) Mem0 has a much broader navigation structure, but it is now a
mature developer/enterprise infrastructure platform with docs, APIs, pricing,
integrations, use cases, enterprise trust, research, and release notes-Fable Sleight is
nowhere near the stage where copying that information architecture helps us. (Mem0)
YC's own design advice also emphasizes reducing competing attention and getting
visitors to the product's “aha” moment quickly. (Y Combinator)

The density problem also improves automatically
With ten pages we were already starting to repeat:
Stage G/G+ on System, Roadmap and Compare.
Frontier research on System and Roadmap.
Capability status on Capabilities and Roadmap.
Trust inside System plus planned Trust page.
That repetition creates two risks:

1. the visitor gets tired;
2. six months later one page says something slightly different from another and the
   site starts contradicting itself.

So v0.8 should introduce content ownership.
For example:

  Full Stage G explanation exists only on Build.

System may say:

   Active research: intelligent recollection →



                             🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                      359 / 456
but clicking it opens Build.
Likewise:

  Full competitive claim exists only on Compare.

  Full privacy/authority explanation exists only on System.

One authoritative public explanation per concept.
That should be part of our density test.


I have rewritten the site architecture around this five-page model:
Download/read the consolidated v0.8 site architecture
So I would not build the previously planned Research, Trust, Product, Lab, or
About pages anymore.
The next useful step is actually to refactor what already exists before adding anything
else:
System gets trimmed. Roadmap becomes BUILD and absorbs Research +
Lab. Capabilities absorbs Product. Compare remains. Comic remains
untouched.
That leaves us with a compact site where every click genuinely takes the visitor
somewhere new instead of restating the same vision.
